Frequently Asked Questions
What is the difference between Community and Pro?
Community Edition is free for individuals and runs the same local transcription engine as Pro. Pro adds a custom model connector, auto-detect meetings, calendar integration, speaker diarization, custom summary templates, and priority support.
Is the Community Edition free?
Yes! The Community Edition is completely free with no hidden costs. Includes real-time transcription, AI summaries, and 100% local processing.
What platforms are supported?
Meetily ships native desktop apps for Windows (EXE) and macOS (.dmg). Both Community and Pro versions support both platforms. For Linux today, build the app bundle from source via the open-source repo (github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily); a native Linux desktop app is in development - join the waitlist at /granola-for-linux.
Do I need internet to use Meetily?
Transcription runs locally on your device by default. For summaries, you choose: local AI (offline) or your own API key (BYOK). Internet is only needed for download, optional updates, and if you choose a cloud-based summary provider.
Is my data safe?
Yes! All processing happens locally on your device. No data is sent to external servers. Your meetings and transcripts stay private.

Privacy-First Recording
Bot-Free Meeting Recording: No Bots Join Your Calls

Bot-free meeting recording captures audio directly from your device without any AI participant joining your call. Meetily transcribes locally using on-device AI, keeping conversations private and meeting flow natural. Works with Zoom, Teams, Google Meet, and any other platform.

Bot-free meeting recording, answered

Last updated: May 9, 2026 • Reviewed by the Meetily open-source team

“Whisper approaches human level robustness and accuracy on English speech recognition… we release the inference code and pre-trained models.”

— Radford et al., “Robust Speech Recognition via Large-Scale Weak Supervision” (OpenAI, 2022). Whisper is one of two transcription engines that ship with Meetily for fully on-device recording.
How to record meetings without a bot

Install a desktop recorder that captures system audio from your own machine instead of joining your call as a participant. Open Meetily on macOS or Windows, click record, then start your Zoom, Microsoft Teams, or Google Meet call as normal. The app pulls audio from the same stream that reaches your speakers, so no AI participant ever appears in the attendee list. Transcription runs locally with Whisper or Parakeet models. 599,000+ users have downloaded Meetily for exactly this workflow as of May 2026.

How to build a botless meeting recorder

A botless recorder needs three pieces: an OS-level system audio capture layer (CoreAudio tap on macOS, WASAPI loopback on Windows), a mic capture stream mixed against it, and a local speech-to-text engine. OpenAI's Whisper paper (2022) showed multilingual ASR can run fully on-device with strong accuracy across 99 languages, which is why Meetily ships Whisper plus Parakeet as the two engines. The full reference implementation is open source under MIT at github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily (31K+ stars, 3,391+ forks as of May 2026).

How to record and transcribe Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams without a bot in 2026

Use a system-audio recorder rather than a meeting-app integration. The same workflow covers all three platforms because Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams all route call audio through your operating system's audio stack. Open Meetily, grant the OS permission to capture system audio (Screen Recording on macOS, none on Windows), then start your call. Audio captures and transcribes locally on your device. No bot is added to the meeting, no platform integration is configured, and the same binary works across all three platforms in 2026.

How to record a meeting without a bot while you are in the meeting

Click record on your desktop recorder before joining, or any time during the call. Because Meetily captures audio from your local system rather than joining as a participant, recording starts and stops without notifying the host or other attendees. There is no participant list change, no chime, no banner. Your microphone and the inbound system audio are both captured, so the full conversation is on the transcript. Transcription updates live during the call and the final transcript and AI summary are available the moment you stop recording.

How to record Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams audio locally without a bot

Local recording means audio bytes never leave your device. Meetily writes the raw audio file to a folder on your machine and runs Whisper or Parakeet on the same machine to produce the transcript. Nothing is uploaded for transcription. For summaries you choose between a local Ollama model or your own Claude or OpenAI API key (transcript text only, no audio, opt-in per meeting). The Community Edition is MIT-licensed and free; Pro is $10/user/month billed annually with priority support.

What is bot-free?

Bot-free, in the meeting recording context, means an AI note-taker that does not join your call as a visible participant. Bot-based tools (Otter, Fireflies, Read.ai) dial in to your meeting under a name like “Notetaker” or “Fathom Notetaker”, which appears in the participant list and uploads audio to vendor cloud servers. Bot-free tools record audio from your own device through the operating system, so no third-party participant is added and no audio leaves your machine for transcription. The term gained search traction during 2025 alongside privacy-first meeting tooling.

What is a bot-free meeting recorder?

A bot-free meeting recorder is a desktop application that records and transcribes meeting audio without sending an AI participant to your call. It captures system audio (the audio your computer plays through speakers) and microphone audio locally, then runs speech recognition on the device. Examples in the open-source space include Meetily, which is MIT-licensed with 31K+ GitHub stars and 599,000+ downloads as of May 2026. The category is distinct from bot-based recorders (Otter, Fireflies, Read.ai) which dial into the call as a visible participant and upload audio to vendor servers.

Sources: OpenAI Whisper paper (Radford et al., 2022); Meetily open-source repository (MIT); Whisper language coverage (~99 languages). Stats verified May 2026 against src/lib/constants.ts.

What Is the Difference Between Bot-Based and Bot-Free Recording?

Bot-based recorders add an AI participant to your calls. Bot-free recorders capture audio from your device. Here is what that means in practice.

Bot-Based Recording
✗
AI bot joins as a visible meeting participant
✗
All attendees see the bot in the participant list
✗
Audio uploaded to cloud servers for processing
✗
Requires platform-specific integrations
✗
Meeting hosts can block or remove the bot
✗
Changes meeting dynamics and participant behavior
Bot-Free Recording (Meetily)
✓
No bot or AI participant joins the meeting
✓
Completely invisible to other attendees
✓
Audio processed locally on your device
✓
Works with any meeting platform automatically
✓
Cannot be blocked by hosts or IT admins
✓
Zero disruption to natural meeting flow
Feature comparison: bot-free recording (Meetily) vs bot-based meeting recorders
Feature	Bot-Free (Meetily)	Bot-Based Recorders
Meeting Participant	No bot joins	Bot visible to all
Data Processing	Local transcription	Cloud servers
Platform Support	Any platform	Integration required
Host Can Block	No	Yes
Meeting Disruption	Zero	Bot notifications
Offline Support	Full offline	Requires internet
Source Code	Open source (MIT)	Proprietary
Cost	Free (Community)	Typically $10-40/month
Who Benefits From Bot-Free Meeting Recording?

Bot-free recording solves real problems for professionals who need privacy, reliability, and seamless meeting experiences

Healthcare & Legal Teams

Record patient consultations, legal depositions, and privileged conversations without a visible bot that could compromise confidentiality or create compliance concerns.

Executive & Board Meetings

Capture strategy sessions, investor calls, and board discussions without alerting participants to recording. Sensitive business decisions stay private and local.

Sales & Client Calls

Record prospect calls without the awkwardness of a 'Notetaker Bot' joining and changing the conversation dynamic. Focus on the relationship, not the tool.

Remote & Hybrid Teams

Works with any meeting platform your team uses - Zoom, Teams, Google Meet, WebEx, Slack, or any other tool. No integrations to configure or maintain.

Regulated Industries

Financial services, government, and defense organizations need recording solutions that keep data on-premises. Bot-free local processing meets strict data residency requirements.

Individual Professionals

Freelancers, consultants, and researchers who need meeting notes without paying $10-40/month. Meetily Community Edition is free and open source.
How Does Bot-Free Meeting Recording Work?

Three steps to silent, private meeting recording

1. Download Meetily

Install the desktop app for Windows or macOS. No account creation, no sign-up, no cloud setup required. Ready to use in under 2 minutes.

2. Join Your Meeting Normally

Start your Zoom, Teams, or Google Meet call as usual. Click record in Meetily. No bot joins your call - recording is completely invisible to other participants.

3. Get Transcription & Notes

Meetily transcribes audio locally using AI models on your device. Review your transcript, get AI-generated summaries, and export notes. With local AI, everything stays on your machine.

Is bot-free recording legal?
Recording laws vary by jurisdiction. In one-party consent regions you can record calls you participate in. In two-party consent regions all parties must agree. Bot-free recording does not change the legal requirements - it simply removes the visible bot that alerts others. As the EFF has long argued in surveillance law commentary, the legality of recording turns on consent rules in your jurisdiction, not on the visibility of the recording tool. Always follow your local recording laws and organizational policies.
Can meeting hosts block bot-free recording?
No. Unlike bot-based recorders that can be blocked by meeting hosts or IT administrators, bot-free recording captures audio from your own device. There is no external participant to block. This makes it reliable for every meeting regardless of platform settings.
Is Meetily's bot-free recording suitable for confidential meetings?
Yes. Bot-free recording is suited for confidential meetings - legal consultations, medical discussions, executive strategy sessions, HR conversations, and financial reviews. No bot joins the call, and audio never leaves your device. When using local AI models, no third party accesses your data. Meetily supports GDPR and HIPAA compliance by design.
Is Meetily free to use for bot-free recording?
Yes. Meetily Community Edition is free and open source under the MIT license. It includes bot-free recording, local transcription, and AI summaries at no cost. Pro is available at $10/user/month billed annually for additional features like advanced summary options and priority support.

Speaker diarization, answered

Last updated: July 22, 2026 • Reviewed by the Meetily team

What is speaker diarization?

Speaker diarization is the process of partitioning an audio recording by speaker - answering “who spoke when” - so a transcript reads as attributed dialogue instead of one undifferentiated block of text. It groups the speech segments that belong to the same voice and labels each one (Speaker 1, Speaker 2, or a real name). It is distinct from speech recognition, which turns audio into words; diarization adds the layer of who said each of those words. In meeting notes it is what turns a raw transcript into a readable record of a conversation.

How does Meetily label who said what?

Meetily Pro identifies each speaker and labels every segment of the transcript with who said it - live as you record, and on audio you import or re-transcribe. It runs on-device, so no audio is uploaded to attribute speakers: the same local pipeline that transcribes with Whisper or Parakeet also handles diarization. Rename a speaker once and the name updates across the whole transcript, and merge duplicates when one person gets split into two labels. Speaker diarization arrived in Meetily Pro 1.8.2.

Speaker diarization vs. speaker identification

Speaker diarization separates a recording into distinct speakers without knowing who they are - it produces anonymous labels like Speaker 1 and Speaker 2. Speaker identification goes a step further and matches a voice to a known identity. In practice, product interfaces use the terms interchangeably: Meetily diarizes each meeting into separate speakers, then lets you name them once so the label carries across the transcript. No enrolled voiceprint database is required - you stay in control of who each speaker is.

Open-source speaker diarization in 2026

Open-source diarization usually means assembling a pipeline yourself - for example OpenAI Whisper for transcription plus pyannote.audio or NVIDIA NeMo for speaker segmentation - which takes setup, tuning, and GPU know-how. Meetily takes a packaged approach: its open-core app pairs on-device Whisper/Parakeet transcription with built-in diarization in the Pro edition, so you get who-said-what labeling without wiring a pipeline together. Everything runs locally on Windows or macOS.
Open Source & Privacy-First

How Does Meetily’s Speaker Diarization Compare?

Most meeting tools offer speaker labels, but they run in the cloud. Whisper on its own does not diarize at all. Here is how the approaches differ.

Speaker diarization: Meetily vs cloud meeting tools vs a raw Whisper setup
Capability	Meetily Pro	Cloud tools (Otter, Fireflies, Granola, Fathom)	Raw Whisper
Speaker diarization	Yes	Yes	No (needs pyannote/NeMo)
Where it runs	On-device	Vendor cloud	Your own setup
Live during the call	Yes	Varies	No (batch only)
On imported audio	Yes	Varies	Yes (if configured)
Rename once, updates everywhere	Yes	Varies	No (manual)
Setup required	None (built in)	Account + bot	Pipeline assembly
Source code	Open-core (MIT app)	Proprietary	Open source

Compare Meetily directly with Otter, Fireflies, and Granola.
How Does Meetily’s Speaker Diarization Work?

Automatic attribution you can correct in seconds

1. Record or Import

Record a live call, or import an existing audio file. Meetily transcribes on-device and attributes each segment to a speaker as it goes - or across the whole file for imports and re-transcriptions.

2. Name Your Speakers

Meetily separates the conversation into distinct voices. Rename a speaker once - from Speaker 2 to a real name - and the label updates across the entire transcript automatically.

3. Merge & Refine

If one person was split into two labels, merge them into a single speaker. Your final transcript reads as clean, attributed dialogue - and feeds clearer, speaker-aware summaries.
Who Needs Speaker Diarization?

Anywhere it matters who said what, on the record

Legal Teams

Depositions, intake calls, and multi-party negotiations where every line has to be attributed to the right person - captured locally to protect privilege.

Healthcare & Therapy

Separate clinician from patient in consultation notes and SOAP documentation, with attribution that stays on your own device.

Government & Finance

Hearings, public meetings, and board minutes that need speaker-attributed records for retention and review - kept in your own infrastructure.

Research & Interviews

User interviews, focus groups, and qualitative research where analysis depends on knowing which participant said what.

Sales & Recruiting

Discovery calls and panel interviews where attributed transcripts make follow-up, coaching, and hand-off notes far clearer.

Distributed Teams

Any team on Zoom, Teams, or Google Meet that wants meeting records reading as a real conversation, not an anonymous transcript.

How does Meetily label who said what?
Meetily Pro identifies each speaker and labels every segment of the transcript with who said it - live as you record and on audio you import or re-transcribe. It runs on-device, so no audio is uploaded to label speakers. Rename a speaker once and the name updates across the entire transcript, and you can merge duplicates if the same person was split into two. Diarization is part of Meetily Pro (since version 1.8.2); the app is open-core with 31K+ GitHub stars.
What is the difference between speaker diarization and speaker identification?
Speaker diarization separates a recording into distinct speakers without knowing who they are - it produces anonymous labels like Speaker 1 and Speaker 2. Speaker identification (or recognition) goes a step further and matches a voice to a known identity. In practice, product UIs use the terms interchangeably: Meetily diarizes each meeting into separate speakers, then lets you name them once so the label carries across the transcript. No enrolled voiceprint database is required.
Does speaker diarization run locally, or does it upload my audio?
In Meetily, diarization runs entirely on-device. Audio never leaves your machine to be split by speaker - the same on-device pipeline that transcribes with Whisper or Parakeet also attributes the speech. This matters for confidential meetings: legal, healthcare, HR, and executive conversations stay on your own hardware. For summaries you separately choose a local model or your own API key (transcript text only, opt-in per meeting).
Does speaker diarization work on imported and re-transcribed audio?
Yes. Meetily attributes speakers live as you record, and also on audio files you import or on past meetings you re-transcribe. Import a recording from any source, run it through Meetily, and the resulting transcript is labeled by speaker. Re-transcribing an older meeting applies diarization to it as well, so your back catalog can gain speaker labels.
What is the best open-source speaker diarization approach in 2026?
Open-source diarization usually means assembling a pipeline yourself - for example OpenAI Whisper for transcription plus pyannote.audio or NVIDIA NeMo for speaker segmentation - which takes setup, tuning, and GPU know-how. Meetily takes a packaged approach: its open-core app pairs on-device Whisper/Parakeet transcription with built-in diarization in the Pro edition, so you get who-said-what labeling without wiring a pipeline together. Everything runs locally on Windows or macOS.
Is speaker diarization included in the free Community Edition?
Speaker diarization is a Meetily Pro feature (introduced in Pro 1.8.2). The free, MIT-licensed Community Edition includes local transcription and AI summaries, but not diarization. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually (early bird) and adds custom summary templates, advanced exports, and priority support. A 14-day trial is available with no card required.
How accurate is on-device speaker diarization?
Diarization accuracy depends on audio quality, how many speakers there are, how much they overlap, and microphone setup - clean, low-overlap audio attributes more reliably than a crowded room on one mic. Meetily lets you correct attribution: rename a speaker once and it propagates, and merge duplicates when one person is split across two labels. That keeps the final transcript accurate even when the automatic pass is imperfect.
Which meeting platforms does speaker diarization work with?
Meetily captures audio from your own device rather than joining the call as a bot, so speaker diarization works the same across Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, and any other platform. There is no per-platform integration to configure. It works on both live calls and imported recordings, on Windows and macOS.
Know Who Said What in Every Meeting

Speaker diarization is in Meetily Pro - $10/user/month billed annually, with a 14-day trial. The free Community Edition covers local transcription and summaries.

Meetily MCP, answered

Last updated: September 25, 2026 • Reviewed by the Meetily team

What is the Meetily MCP server?

MCP (Model Context Protocol) is the open protocol, introduced by Anthropic in 2024, that AI assistants use to connect to other apps. Meetily Pro 1.11.0 ships an official MCP server inside the app, so an assistant on the same computer can work with your meetings: search across transcripts, list meetings, read a transcript or summary, export a meeting, regenerate a summary, and start or stop a recording. What it may do is up to you: a new connection starts read-only, and recording and editing summaries are separate permissions you switch on for each assistant. Deleting meetings is never available through the MCP server.

Which AI assistants work with Meetily?

Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor or Windsurf connect in one click from Meetily’s Integrations tab when they are installed on the same computer. Claude Code and Codex also need their own command-line tool installed for Meetily to detect them. Meetily lists VS Code but cannot connect it from the app yet; VS Code and other MCP clients can be added by hand with the setup guide in the meetily-workflows repository.

Step-by-step guides: connect Claude Desktop or Claude Code, and use your meetings in Cursor, Windsurf or Codex.

What can I connect Meetily to besides an AI assistant?

Your own scripts. The meetily-pro command-line tool and the local HTTP API let a script search meetings, export any meeting as Markdown, text or JSON, and start or stop a recording. Webhooks signed with HMAC-SHA256 tell your script the moment a summary is ready or, with the Record permission, a recording ends.

Code language:
bash
meetily-pro meetings export <meeting-id> --format md

That is how notes reach Notion, Jira, a CRM or an Obsidian vault: your script does the sending. Meetily has no built-in connectors for those apps. See Meetily workflows for recipes.

Where does my meeting data go when an assistant reads it?

Meetily serves your meetings to the assistant on your own computer; the API listens only there by default. What happens next is up to the assistant: most send what they read to their own AI provider, the same as anything else you ask them. Connect the assistants you already trust with your meeting notes, and check how each AI provider handles your data if you are not sure. Transcription itself always runs locally.

Is this the official Meetily MCP server?

Yes. The MCP server on this page is built into Meetily Pro and maintained by Zackriya Solutions, the team behind Meetily. Community members have also built unofficial MCP connectors for Meetily; those are separate projects that work differently and are not covered here.
How Do I Turn On the Meetily MCP Server?

Three steps in Meetily Pro 1.11.0 or later

1. Turn On Integrations

Open Settings > PRO and turn on Integrations. A new Integrations tab appears with the Automation API switch, the one on/off for every assistant and key.

2. Connect and Choose Permissions

Click Connect next to your assistant and choose what it may do. New connections start read-only. Then turn on its Allow switch.

3. Ask About Your Meetings

Restart the assistant so it loads the Meetily tools, then ask things like "What did we decide last week?" or "List my action items from yesterday."
The Integrations tab: one switch for everything, then Connect.
New connections start read-only; Record and Write are switched on here as an example.

Full setup guide: Meetily developer docs

How Does Meetily’s MCP Server Compare?

Some meeting assistants now offer an MCP server. The difference is where your meetings live and what is on by default.

Meetily Pro vs typical cloud meeting assistants

MCP server for AI assistants	Yes, built in	Offered by some
Where your meetings are served from	Your own computer	The vendor's cloud

Signed webhooks	Yes (HMAC-SHA256)	Offered by some
No-code app connectors	No, use your own script	Offered by some
Default state	Off until you turn it on	Varies

If you want ready-made connectors with no scripting, a cloud assistant is the better fit. Compare Meetily with Granola, Fireflies and Otter.

Meetily MCP Server: Common Questions
Is the Meetily MCP server free?
The MCP server, the meetily-pro CLI, the local API and webhooks are Meetily Pro features: $10/user/month billed annually, and they are included in the 14-day Pro trial. The free, MIT-licensed Community Edition records and transcribes meetings locally but does not include them.
Which AI assistants can I connect to Meetily in one click?
Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor or Windsurf, when the assistant is installed on the same computer. Claude Code and Codex also need their own command-line tool installed for Meetily to detect them. VS Code and other assistants that support MCP can be added by hand.
Is the Meetily API turned on by default?
No. The Automation API, the MCP server and webhooks are off until you turn on Integrations in Settings > PRO. By default the API listens only on your own computer, and one switch turns every assistant and key off again.
Can my AI assistant start or stop a recording?
Yes, if you allow it. A new connection starts read-only; recording and editing summaries are separate permissions you switch on per assistant. No assistant can delete meetings through Meetily's MCP server.
Can Meetily send meeting notes to Notion, Slack or my CRM automatically?
Not with a built-in connector. Meetily Pro gives your own script what it needs: a signed webhook when a summary is ready, and a command-line tool and local API that export any meeting as Markdown, text or JSON. The script then sends the notes wherever you want them.
Does Meetily have a Python or JavaScript SDK?
There is no official SDK. Scripts use the meetily-pro command-line tool or call the local HTTP API directly, which works from any language. The meetily-workflows repository includes small example helpers.
Does the Meetily MCP server work with Claude on the web?
No. The Meetily MCP server runs on your own computer, so it works with assistants installed on the same machine, such as Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Windsurf. A browser-based assistant cannot reach it.
How do I check that a webhook really came from Meetily?
Every webhook is signed with HMAC-SHA256 and carries the signature in the X-Meetily-Signature header. Your script checks it with the signing secret that Meetily shows once, when you add the webhook. The developer docs show the exact steps.

Meetily Pro 1.11.0
Meetily Workflows: Send Meeting Notes Where Your Team Works

A Meetily workflow is a short script or an AI assistant that takes your meeting notes somewhere useful: an Obsidian vault, a Notion page, Jira or GitHub issues, a Slack channel or your CRM. Meetily Pro 1.11.0 gives you the pieces (signed webhooks, the meetily-pro CLI, a local API and an MCP server) and your script or assistant does the last step. Meetily has no built-in connectors for these apps.

Start free trial
Example workflows on GitHub
OBSIDIAN
NOTION
JIRA & GITHUB
SLACK & CRM
REACT THE MOMENT A SUMMARY IS READY
EXPORT MARKDOWN, TEXT OR JSON
DESTINATIONS YOU APPROVE IN THE APP
MEETINGS RECORDED AND TRANSCRIBED ON YOUR COMPUTER
How Does a Meetily Workflow Run?

Most workflows follow the same three steps

1. The Meeting Ends

Meetily records and transcribes on your computer. With Auto Summary turned on in Summary settings, it then writes the summary with a local model or your own API key.

2. Meetily Sends a Webhook

A signed summary.completed webhook tells a script on your computer which meeting is ready.

3. Your Script Sends the Notes

The script exports the meeting with meetily-pro and posts it to Obsidian, Notion, Jira, Slack or your CRM.
Workflow recipes

Last updated: September 25, 2026 • Reviewed by the Meetily team

How do I save meeting notes to Obsidian automatically?

Subscribe a small script on your computer to the summary.completed webhook. When it fires, the script takes the meeting ID from the webhook and exports that meeting as Markdown straight into your vault:

Code language:
bash
meetily-pro webhooks add --url http://127.0.0.1:9002/meetily --events summary.completed

# in your script, for the meeting ID the webhook delivered:
meetily-pro meetings export "$MEETING_ID" --format md > ~/Obsidian/Meetings/"$MEETING_ID".md

What you need: Allow the CLI on this computer turned on (Integrations > Apps & scripts) and Auto Summary turned on. Under Integrations > Advanced, turn on Outgoing (webhooks) and add your script’s address as a local target before you add the webhook, then approve the destination when Meetily asks. Meetily must be running.

For a full setup built this way, with a knowledge graph per client and an action-item tracker, read how a consultant built a private second brain on Meetily.

How do I send meeting summaries to Notion?

Same trigger, different destination: when summary.completed arrives, your script exports the meeting and creates a page through Notion’s own API. Or skip the script and ask an AI assistant that has access to both Meetily and Notion to copy yesterday’s summary into your meetings database.

What you need: The webhook setup from the Obsidian recipe (or a connected assistant), and a Notion integration token for your script.

How do I turn meeting action items into Jira, Linear or GitHub issues?

Two routes. Ask a connected AI assistant that also has access to your tracker: “file the action items from today’s sprint planning as issues.” Or export the meeting as JSON in a script and create the issues through your tracker’s API. A custom summary template that always lists owners and action items under the same headings makes the notes easier for a script to parse.

What you need: Read permission. Meetily does not file issues itself; your assistant or script uses the tracker access you gave it.

How do I post a meeting summary to Slack after every meeting?

Have your script listen for summary.completed, read the summary, and post it to a Slack incoming webhook for the channel you choose. The developer docs have a step-by-step Slack summary guide.

What you need: Auto Summary turned on, the webhook setup from the Obsidian recipe, and a Slack incoming webhook URL.

How do I update my CRM after a sales call?

Export the call as JSON when its summary is ready and write the summary and next steps to the contact or deal record with your CRM’s API. If you want this with no scripting at all, a cloud meeting assistant with a native CRM connector is the better fit; Meetily is built for teams that want the recording and transcript to stay on their own machines.

What you need: The webhook setup from the Obsidian recipe, and an API key for your CRM.

How do I back up all my meetings?

List your meetings and export each one as JSON (the default format) to a folder you back up. The developer docs have a backup and batch export guide.

Code language:
bash
meetily-pro meetings list --limit 100 --json
meetily-pro meetings export <meeting-id> > backup/<meeting-id>.json

What you need: Read permission: allowing the CLI in the app is enough. Meetily must be running.
Setup guides with screenshots and example workflows live in the meetily-workflows repository and the developer docs.

New to the pieces? Start with the Meetily MCP server and the Meetily CLI.

Meetily Workflows: Common Questions
Does Meetily have a built-in Notion, Slack or Jira integration?
No. Meetily has no native connectors for those apps. Meetily Pro gives you the pieces instead: signed webhooks, the meetily-pro command-line tool, a local API and an MCP server for AI assistants. Your own script or assistant does the last step.
Do I need to write code?
For webhooks and scripts, yes, usually a short script. For the AI assistant route, no: connect Claude, Codex, Cursor or Windsurf and ask it to do the step, as long as it has access to the other app.
Can a cloud automation service receive Meetily webhooks?
It would not get far. Webhook payloads carry meeting IDs, not the notes, and the API that serves the notes listens only on your own computer, so a service running in the cloud cannot fetch them. Run the script on the same computer as Meetily.
Does Meetily need to be running for a workflow to fire?
Yes. Webhooks come from the running app, and scripts read meetings through its local API, so keep Meetily open with Integrations turned on.
Are workflows included in the Pro trial?
Yes. The webhooks, CLI, local API and MCP server are part of Meetily Pro ($10/user/month billed annually) and are included in the 14-day trial. The free Community Edition does not include them.
Where can I find example scripts?
The meetily-workflows repository on GitHub has setup guides with screenshots and example workflows, and the Meetily developer docs include guides such as posting a summary to Slack and backing up meetings.

Pro FAQ
How long is the Pro trial?
The Pro trial is 14 days with full Pro features and flexible AI summaries (local or BYOK). No credit card required to start. After the trial you get a 1-day grace period, and your trial license is valid for activation within a 14-day window.
Do you offer a refund?
Yes. Pro includes a 30-day money-back guarantee. If Pro is not the right fit, email support within 30 days of purchase and we will refund you in full.
How much does Pro cost?
$10/user/month billed annually (60% off the $25/month regular price). 30-day money-back guarantee.
How are summaries generated and what data is sent?
Summaries are pluggable. Run a local model to keep everything on-device, or bring your own API key (Claude, OpenAI, Groq, OpenRouter, or any OpenAI-compatible endpoint). With BYOK, only the transcript text is sent to your chosen provider. Audio and recordings never leave your device.
How does BYOK (Bring Your Own Key) work?
Bring your own API key from Anthropic, OpenAI, Groq, OpenRouter, or any OpenAI-compatible endpoint. You pay your provider directly, you control data retention and routing. See the per-provider privacy summaries at /llm-privacy for retention and zero-data-retention details.
Does Meetily send audio to the cloud for summaries?
No. Transcription runs locally by default, and on that path audio and recordings never leave your device. For summaries with BYOK, only the transcript text is sent to your chosen AI provider. If you prefer fully local, pair Meetily with Ollama.
Is Pro for individuals or teams?
Both. Pro is licensed per user at $10/user/month billed annually. You can buy a single seat for yourself or multiple seats for a team. For larger orgs with procurement or custom terms, talk to us about Enterprise.
Is Linux supported?
Pro ships native installers for Windows and macOS today. A native Linux build is in development - join the waitlist at /granola-for-linux. The Community Edition can be built from source on Linux today.
Can I cancel anytime?
Yes. You can cancel anytime from your billing portal. Cancellation takes effect at the end of your current annual term, and you retain Pro access until then.
What's the difference between Pro and Community?
Community is free under MIT license with local transcription, AI summaries, audio import, and retranscription. Pro adds flexible AI summaries (local or BYOK), custom summary templates, advanced exports (PDF / DOCX / Markdown), connections to your AI assistant and your own scripts (MCP, webhooks, command-line tool), and email priority support.
What happens after the 14-day trial ends?
When your trial ends you can purchase Pro at $10/user/month billed annually, or fall back to Community Edition (free, MIT) with no data loss. Trial licenses are valid for activation within a 14-day window.
What payment methods do you accept?
Payments are processed via Lemon Squeezy. We accept all major credit and debit cards. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually.

What IT, security, and procurement teams ask before standing up Meetily Enterprise

Can Meetily Enterprise be self-hosted on our own infrastructure?

What does the admin dashboard cover?

What kind of SLA and support do you offer?
Enterprise plans include 24/7 priority support, a named account manager, white-glove deployment assistance, and an SLA matched to the deployment tier. Specific uptime, response, and resolution targets are agreed in the contract; we do not publish a one-size-fits-all SLA because self-hosted deployments depend on your infrastructure.
What does Meetily Enterprise cost?
Custom pricing only - it depends on user count, deployment model (on-prem vs your cloud), compliance framework requirements, and SLA tier. Click 'Talk to Us' on this page to book a demo and we will scope a quote within one business day.

Coming Soon: Linux Native
Granola for Linux: Native Linux App, Coming Soon

Granola has no Linux app today. Open-source Meetily Pro is bringing native Linux support. Join the waitlist to get notified when it ships.

Why Linux native matters

A Linux desktop app gives you the same on-device privacy benefits Linux users already expect from the rest of their stack.

On-device transcription

Audio capture and transcription run entirely on your machine. No bot joins the call. No transcript leaves your device unless you choose BYOK for summaries.

No admin permissions required

A native Linux app installs into your home directory. No sudo prompt, no kernel modules, no system-wide changes. Your IT team can audit the binary and the source.

Runs offline

Once Whisper / parakeet models are downloaded, transcription works with zero network access. Useful in air-gapped environments and on flights.
Linux options today: a three-way comparison

Honest framing of what each option ships today.

Capability	Granola native	Meetily Linux native	Meetily build-from-source
Native Linux desktop app	No	In development (waitlist)	Yes (build the app bundle yourself)
Available today	No (macOS / Windows only)	No	Yes
On-device transcription	Hybrid local / cloud	Yes (planned at launch)	Yes
Source code	Proprietary	Open source (MIT)	Open source (MIT)
Best for	Mac / Windows users only	Linux desktop users (later)	Linux desktop users today (DIY build)

Granola facts as of May 2026. Granola positions itself as a Mac and Windows desktop app with cloud sync; its docs do not list a Linux desktop build.
What Meetily does, and what Linux native unlocks

Meetily is an open-source AI meeting note taker. Audio is captured directly from your device (no bot joins the call), transcription runs locally with Whisper or parakeet models, and you choose how summaries are generated: a local model via Ollama or your own API key with Claude or GPT.

On macOS and Windows that experience ships as a native desktop app. On Linux today, you can build the app bundle from source via the open-source repo (github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily). The native Linux desktop app is what is in development - the waitlist below is how we prioritize distros and ship order.

Native Linux unlocks the same workflow Linux users already expect: drop the binary in your home directory, run it without sudo, capture audio with the same on-device privacy guarantees as the macOS and Windows builds. Tell us your distro on the form below and we'll prioritize accordingly.

The plan is feature parity with the macOS and Windows builds at launch: real-time transcription, speaker identification, summaries, custom AI connectors, and recordings saved locally. Anything with a platform-specific dependency (like macOS-only system audio capture) will be called out in the release notes.

Linux waitlist FAQ
When will Meetily Linux native ship?
Native Linux is in development. We're not committing to a public ship date until the build is reproducible across major distros and our installer matrix is green. Join the waitlist and we'll email you the moment it ships.
Which Linux distributions will be supported?
We're targeting Ubuntu 22.04+ first because it covers the largest share of Linux desktop users. Debian, Fedora, and Arch are on the evaluation list. Tell us your distro on the waitlist form so we can prioritize.
Will the Linux build be open source?
Yes. The Meetily Community Edition is MIT-licensed (github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily). The Linux build will follow the same MIT license at launch. Pro features remain in the Pro tier.
Can I run Meetily on Linux today?
Yes. Build the app bundle from the open-source repo (github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily) - the native Linux desktop app is what is in development.
Will Pro features work on Linux at launch?
Yes. The goal is feature parity with macOS and Windows at the Pro tier on Linux at launch. We'll call out any feature with a platform-specific dependency in the release notes.
Will Meetily for Linux support ARM (Raspberry Pi, ARM servers)?
ARM Linux is on the evaluation list but is not a launch blocker. We'll start with x86_64 to match where most Linux desktop users are today, then evaluate ARM based on waitlist demand.
Will GPU acceleration work on Linux?
GPU acceleration is part of the evaluation. Whisper.cpp and parakeet support CUDA on Linux upstream, and we're testing the integration. We won't block launch on GPU but will ship CPU support first.
How does Pro pricing on Linux compare to Granola?
Meetily Pro will be $10/user/month billed annually on Linux at launch, the same price as on macOS and Windows. Community Edition (MIT, free) will also support Linux at launch. Granola does not currently ship a Linux desktop app.
We'll let you know the moment Linux native is ready

No spam, one email when it ships. In the meantime, the open-source repo is live on GitHub.

Frequently Asked Questions

Everything you need to know about Meetily pricing

What happens after my trial ends?
After 14 days, Pro features will be disabled but your data remains safe on your device. You can continue using Community features for free, or purchase a Pro license to restore full functionality. There's also a 1-day grace period after the trial ends.
Can I use Meetily completely free?
Yes! Meetily Community Edition is free and open source. Transcription runs on your device, and summaries run on a local model by default or through your own API key. Pro adds naming your speakers, cloud and custom transcription providers, and advanced exports.
How do I activate my license?
Open Meetily and follow the setup steps; at the license step, paste your license key and click Activate. It takes just seconds. Your trial key is shown on screen when you request it, and paid keys arrive by email after purchase. Trial keys must be activated within 14 days of generation.
What payment methods do you accept?
We accept all major credit cards (Visa, Mastercard, American Express), PayPal, and Apple Pay through our secure payment partner LemonSqueezy. All transactions are encrypted and secure.
Can I get a refund?
Yes, we offer a 30-day money-back guarantee. If you're not satisfied with Meetily Pro, contact us at meetily@zackriya.com for a full refund, no questions asked.
Do I need an internet connection?
Meetily transcription runs locally on your device. You need internet for license activation, downloading the app, and downloading local AI models. Recordings and audio never leave your device. For summaries, you choose your provider: local AI or your own API key (BYOK).
What's the difference between Pro Trial and Pro?
Pro Trial gives you full access to all Pro features for 14 days at no cost. After the trial, you can purchase a Pro license ($120/year) to continue using Pro features. The only difference is the duration - trial is 14 days, paid is 1 year.
Can I use the same license on multiple devices?
Each Pro license is valid for one device. If you need licenses for multiple devices or team members, contact us about Enterprise plans which offer volume licensing with discounts.

What you get, end to end

Six features that matter to anyone searching for an open source AI note taker.

MIT license

Read it, fork it, ship it inside a commercial product. No copyleft, no attribution clause, no field-of-use restriction.

100% local transcription

Audio is transcribed on your device with local Whisper or Parakeet models. Nothing uploaded for the transcript step, ever.

99+ languages

Multilingual transcription out of the box. Mixed-language meetings and accented speech both supported.

No internet required for recording or transcription. Useful on flights, in SCIFs, behind air-gapped networks, or when the wifi is just bad.

How it differs from the cloud incumbents

Granola, Otter, and Fireflies are all good products. They are also all closed source and rely on the cloud for some part of the pipeline. Here is the honest delta.

Open Source AI Note Taker: FAQ
Is Meetily really open source?
Yes. The Meetily Community Edition is fully open source. The source code lives at github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily, anyone can read it, fork it, audit it, and contribute back. Releases ship as signed Windows and macOS installers built from the same public repo.
What's the license?
MIT License. You can use Meetily commercially, fork it, modify it, and redistribute it. The only requirement is keeping the copyright notice. There is no copyleft, no attribution clause, no field-of-use restriction.
Does it run offline?
Yes for transcription. Meetily transcribes meetings on your device using local Whisper or Parakeet models, so audio never leaves your machine and you can record without an internet connection. Summaries are pluggable: choose a local model (Ollama or the built-in summarizer) to stay fully offline, or bring your own key (BYOK) for Claude, OpenAI, or Groq on Pro.
How is it different from Granola?
Granola is closed source and syncs meeting data to its cloud by default. Meetily is MIT-licensed open source with 100% local transcription, no cloud sync, and a build-it-yourself path if you want full control. See the detailed feature-by-feature comparison at /vs/granola.
How is it different from Otter and Fireflies?
Otter and Fireflies are cloud SaaS products. They upload your audio, transcribe in their data center, and store transcripts on their servers. They also rely on meeting bots that join calls as a separate participant. Meetily records system audio locally with no bot joining, transcribes on your device, and lets you keep summaries local too. Full comparisons at /vs/otter-ai and /vs/fireflies.
Can I self-host Meetily?
The desktop app already runs entirely on your own device, which is the most common self-host shape. For Enterprise deployments that want a managed server (centralized backups, retention policies), contact meetily@zackriya.com for a self-hosted package.
Is there a commercial version?
Yes. Meetily Pro adds advanced summary options, priority support, advanced exports, and team features on top of the open-source Community Edition. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually (regular $25/month). The Community Edition stays free under MIT with no usage caps.
Is it on GitHub?
Yes. The repository is github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily. Star it, file issues, or send a pull request. Releases, changelogs, and the public roadmap all live there.
What about Linux?
Today: build the app bundle from source via the open-source repo (instructions in the README). A native Linux desktop app is in development; join the waitlist at /granola-for-linux to be notified when it ships. Pro on Linux at launch will be the same tier as macOS and Windows.
If I use Pro's BYOK summaries, what about the LLM provider's privacy policy?
We publish a vendor-verified data-retention summary for every cloud LLM Meetily supports, plus the local Ollama path that keeps summaries on-device. Start at /llm-privacy for the index, then read /llm-privacy/anthropic, /llm-privacy/openai, or /llm-privacy/ollama for per-provider zero-data-retention (ZDR), training-default, and retention specifics. Audio never leaves your device regardless of which summary path you pick - only transcript text is sent to a cloud LLM, and only when you choose BYOK.

Frequently Asked Questions

Common questions about Meetily's GDPR Article 9, DSGVO, RGPD, and HIPAA compliance for European and US healthcare organizations

Is Meetily HIPAA compliant for US healthcare organizations?
Meetily is HIPAA compliant by design, not formally certified - HIPAA does not issue product certifications. Transcription runs 100% on the clinician's device via Whisper, so PHI never leaves your network. This satisfies the HIPAA Security Rule §164.312 technical safeguards (access control, audit controls, integrity, transmission security) for the transcription path. A signed Business Associate Agreement (BAA) is available on the Enterprise tier when using a cloud summary provider (BYOK); if you stay on local-only summarization, no BAA with us is required because no PHI ever reaches our infrastructure.
Does Meetily use a meeting bot that joins the call?
No. Meetily captures system audio directly on the clinician's machine. No bot joins the meeting, no third-party participant appears in your Zoom / Teams / Meet attendee list, and patients are not prompted to consent to a recording bot. This is the bot-free design searchers ask about - the audio path stays inside the device that's already authorized to be in the visit.
Is Meetily GDPR compliant for European healthcare organizations?
Yes, Meetily is GDPR compliant by design, aligned with GDPR Article 9 requirements for special category data (health data). With 100% local transcription, patient data never leaves your EU infrastructure, supporting compliance with GDPR, DSGVO (Germany), RGPD (France), and national healthcare data protection laws across EU member states. No cross-border data transfers, no DPA needed with us for the transcription path.
How does Meetily comply with DSGVO requirements for German hospitals?
Meetily satisfies German DSGVO (Datenschutz-Grundverordnung) and BDSG requirements through local processing architecture. All patient consultation recordings and transcriptions stay within your German infrastructure (on-premise or German cloud like Hetzner), eliminating Article 44 cross-border transfer risks. Meets requirements from Bundesbeauftragter für den Datenschutz (BfDI) and Landesdatenschutzbeauftragte for healthcare data processing.
Can French healthcare providers use Meetily for RGPD compliance?
Absolutely. Meetily meets French RGPD (Règlement Général sur la Protection des Données) and CNIL guidelines for healthcare data. Deploy on French infrastructure (OVH cloud or on-premise) to ensure patient data stays in France. Satisfies CNIL's strict data minimization requirements and healthcare-specific deliberations for patient consultation recordings.
How does Meetily protect patient data across EU and US healthcare regulations?
Meetily uses a universal privacy-by-design approach that satisfies both EU GDPR and US HIPAA requirements simultaneously. Local processing ensures patient data never leaves your healthcare network, meeting GDPR Article 32 (security of processing), Article 25 (privacy by design), and HIPAA §164.312 technical safeguards. Deploy in your region (EU or US) for automatic compliance with local healthcare data protection laws.
Can Meetily be used for telemedicine consultations in Europe?
Yes, Meetily is perfect for European telemedicine and remote healthcare consultations. It provides GDPR Article 9 compliant documentation for patient consultations across all EU member states. Works with popular platforms (Zoom, Teams, Google Meet) while keeping all patient health data within your EU infrastructure, meeting MDR (Medical Device Regulation) and eHealth requirements.
What languages does Meetily support for European healthcare?
Meetily supports 99+ languages including 27 EU languages with medical terminology recognition, such as: German (Deutsch), French (Français), Spanish (Español), Italian (Italiano), Dutch (Nederlands), Portuguese (Português), Polish (Polski), Swedish (Svenska), and more. High accuracy for medical consultations in all major EU languages, perfect for multilingual healthcare organizations.
How does Meetily handle MDR compliance for medical device software?
Meetily's architecture supports EU Medical Device Regulation (MDR) compliance as it processes all patient data locally without cloud dependencies. Healthcare organizations can classify Meetily according to their specific use case risk assessment. The local processing architecture provides the foundation for meeting MDR Annex I requirements for medical software involving patient data.

Frequently Asked Questions

Common questions about Meetily's attorney-client privilege protection, ethics compliance, and legal features

How does Meetily protect attorney-client privilege?
Meetily transcribes meetings 100% on the attorney's device using Whisper. There is no cloud upload of the audio or transcript by default, no third-party meeting bot joining the call, and no vendor-controlled data residency. Privilege stays within the firm because the privileged communication never crosses our infrastructure on the transcription path. If you choose a cloud summary provider (BYOK) on the Pro tier, only the transcript text you opt in to send is processed - audio and recordings always stay on the device.
Does a meeting bot join client calls or depositions?
No. Meetily captures system audio directly on the laptop. There is no third-party participant in the Zoom / Teams / Webex attendee list, which avoids both the optics problem (opposing counsel sees a 'Meeting Bot' in the room) and the privilege problem (a third-party processor receiving the audio stream).
What audit trail does Meetily provide for legal records?
On Community and Pro, recordings, transcripts, and timestamps are saved locally and can be archived under your existing matter-management system. On Enterprise, an admin dashboard adds centralized audit logs (who recorded what, when, on which device) and retention controls so the firm - not a vendor - decides how long material is kept and who can access it.
Is Meetily compliant with bar association requirements?
Yes, Meetily is designed to comply with bar association ethical requirements for client confidentiality and data protection. The local processing architecture ensures you maintain complete control over client communications and work product.
Can Meetily be used for client consultations?
Yes, Meetily is ideal for client consultations. It provides secure, confidential documentation while maintaining attorney-client privilege. The system handles initial consultations, client meetings, and case discussions with complete privacy.
How is data sovereignty maintained?
Data sovereignty is maintained through local processing - all data processing happens within your own infrastructure, not on external servers. This ensures complete control over privileged communications and client information.
What security measures protect client confidentiality?
Meetily implements multiple security measures: AES-256 encryption, secure authentication, role-based access control, detailed audit trails, and automatic privileged communication detection. All processing happens locally within your secure environment.

Frequently Asked Questions

Common questions about Meetily's SEC & FINRA compliance, financial data protection, and regulatory features

How does Meetily support SEC 17a-4 and FINRA record-keeping?
Meetily produces immutable local recordings and timestamped transcripts of client and advisory meetings, which can be archived under your existing WORM-compliant storage to support SEC Rule 17a-4(f) electronic record requirements and FINRA Rule 4511 record-retention obligations. Compliance is implemented by the firm's record-keeping policy and storage layer; Meetily provides the audit-grade source artifacts. We do not claim independent SEC or FINRA certification - those are firm-level designations.
Does Meetily support MiFID II call recording for European advisors?
Yes. MiFID II Article 16(7) requires recording of communications that result in or may result in a transaction. Meetily records and transcribes client investment meetings 100% on the advisor's device, with the recording artifact retained under the firm's MiFID II retention policy (5 years, extensible to 7 by national regulator). Combined with on-device processing, the recording stays within the firm's control - no third-party cloud processor is in the loop.
Is Meetily SOC 2 certified?
Meetily is not formally SOC 2 certified - the architecture supports SOC 2 controls (access control on Enterprise, audit logging, encryption in transit and at rest), but no current third-party attestation report exists. For institutions that require a SOC 2 report from the vendor, our recommendation today is the self-hosted Enterprise deployment, which puts the trust boundary inside your existing audited environment.
What security measures protect financial data?
Transcription runs locally on the advisor's own device, so recordings and transcripts are never sent to Meetily - there is no server of ours holding them. Role-based access control, detailed audit trails, and compliance flagging come with Enterprise, where meetings are stored centrally on your own infrastructure.
Can Meetily be used for client advisory meetings?
Yes, Meetily is ideal for client advisory meetings. It provides secure, compliant documentation while maintaining complete confidentiality. The system handles investment reviews, advisory sessions, and client consultations with full regulatory compliance.
How is data sovereignty maintained?
Data sovereignty is maintained through local processing - all data processing happens within your own infrastructure, not on external servers. This ensures complete control over client information and compliance with financial regulations.
What compliance features are built into Meetily?
Enterprise deployments add automatic audit trails, retention policy enforcement, role-based access control, compliance flagging, and detailed activity logging, running on your own infrastructure. That architecture is built to support SEC Rule 17a-4 and FINRA record-keeping requirements - Meetily is not certified against them, and the controls remain your firm's to configure and evidence.

Frequently Asked Questions

Common questions about Meetily's FedRAMP-ready architecture, data sovereignty, and local-processing security model

Is Meetily FedRAMP-authorized or FISMA-certified?
No. Meetily is not FedRAMP-authorized and holds no FISMA certification. Authorization is a customer or agency designation for a specific deployment, not a vendor badge. What Meetily provides is a FedRAMP-ready architecture that supports FISMA controls by design and aligns with the NIST 800-53 control families underpinning those programs - with on-device, fully local transcription. Your agency runs and accredits the deployment inside its own environment.
How does Meetily help protect sensitive information?
Meetily runs inside your own accredited or air-gapped environment. Transcription happens 100% locally on the agency device, so meeting audio never leaves your infrastructure. There is no meeting bot joining the call. Access controls, audit logging, and classification handling remain the responsibility of your accredited environment.
How is FOIA record-keeping supported?
Meetily produces local transcripts and summaries that your agency can retain as records. Recordings and transcripts are saved locally to the agency device, and advanced export formats support FOIA-ready record-keeping inside your own systems.
How is data sovereignty maintained?
Data sovereignty is maintained through local processing - transcription happens on your own infrastructure, not on external servers. This gives your agency full control over government meeting data.
How does Meetily align with government security requirements?
Meetily's architecture aligns with the NIST 800-53 control families that underpin FedRAMP and FISMA, and supports FISMA controls by design. Recordings stay on the agency device, transcription runs entirely on-device, and self-hosted or air-gapped deployment keeps data inside your environment. Meetily is not FedRAMP-authorized; authorization is a designation your agency obtains for its specific deployment.

Bot-Free Meeting Notes for Engineering and Product Teams

Meetily is a bot-free AI meeting assistant for engineering and product teams who want standups, sprint planning and incident reviews transcribed on their own machines. It records your computer's audio on Windows or macOS, so no bot joins the Zoom, Teams or Google Meet call, and Whisper or Parakeet transcribes it 100% locally. With Meetily Pro, your own script or AI assistant can read the summary and file the action items as Jira, Linear or GitHub issues.

Built for the Meetings Engineering Teams Actually Have

Standups, sprint planning, incident reviews and architecture discussions, captured without a bot and transcribed on the machine that recorded them.

No Bot in the Call

Meetily records your computer's system audio, so it works with Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, Slack Huddles, Discord and Webex. Nobody has to admit a recorder into the standup.

Transcription Stays on Your Laptop

Whisper or Parakeet runs 100% locally on Windows and macOS (Apple Silicon), so roadmap talk and unreleased architecture never reach a transcription vendor. For summaries you pick a local model or your own API key.

Who Said What, in the Shape You Need

Pro's speaker diarization labels each speaker, and custom summary templates shape the notes for a sprint planning, retro or incident review. Export to Markdown, PDF or DOCX.
Send Action Items to Jira, Linear or GitHub With Your Own Script or AI Assistant

Meetily Pro 1.11.0 added four ways for your tools to reach your meetings. Meetily has no native Jira connector and does not file tickets itself: your script or assistant does the writing, with the tracker access you already gave it.

AI Assistants over MCP

Connect Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Cursor or Windsurf in one click. The assistant can search your meetings, read transcripts and summaries, and start or stop a recording. Ask it to turn yesterday's sprint planning into issues, and if it has its own Jira, Linear or GitHub access, it can file them.

Signed Webhooks

Get an HMAC-SHA256 signed webhook when a recording ends or a summary is ready. A script on your machine verifies the signature, pulls the summary and opens GitHub issues for the action items.

The meetily-pro CLI

Bundled with Pro. meetily-pro meetings export <id> --format md writes a meeting to Markdown (txt and json also work), ready to pipe into the script that calls your tracker's API.

A Local HTTP API

The API listens only on your computer by default. Your scripts read meetings and summaries locally, and nothing goes to Jira, Linear or GitHub until your script sends it.

Off Until You Turn It On

Nothing is connected until you enable the Automation API in Settings > PRO. Each assistant gets its own permissions and each API key its own scopes. Disconnect or revoke any time.

Templates Your Script Can Parse

A custom summary template for standups or sprint planning asks for decisions, owners and action items under the same headings every time, which makes the notes easier for the script that files issues to parse.
Open Source, Built in Public

The Community Edition is MIT licensed on GitHub, so your team can read how the open source app captures and transcribes meeting audio.

Frequently Asked Questions

How Meetily fits standups, sprint planning and incident reviews, and how action items get to Jira, Linear or GitHub.

Does Meetily integrate with Jira?
Not natively. Meetily has no built-in Jira connector, and it does not create tickets on its own. Since Meetily Pro 1.11.0 your own tools can do it: an AI assistant connected through Meetily's MCP server (Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Cursor or Windsurf) can read a meeting's summary and file the action items using its own Jira access, or a script can export the meeting with the meetily-pro CLI and call the Jira API. The same approach works for Linear and GitHub Issues.
How do I turn meeting action items into Jira, Linear or GitHub issues?
Turn on the Automation API in Settings > PRO, then pick a route. Connect your AI assistant over MCP and ask it to create issues from a meeting's action items. Or have a script on your machine listen for the summary-ready webhook, which is signed with HMAC-SHA256 so the script can verify it. Or run meetily-pro meetings export <id> --format md (txt and json also work) and parse the output. Your script or assistant does the writing, so you decide the project, labels and assignees.
Does a bot join our standups or sprint planning calls?
No. Meetily records your computer's system audio, so nothing joins the Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, Slack Huddle, Discord or Webex call and the participant list looks the same as always. Let your team know when you are recording.
Does code or roadmap discussion leave the engineer's laptop?
Transcription runs 100% locally with Whisper or Parakeet, and recordings are saved on your computer. For summaries you choose: a local model keeps everything on the device, while your own API key (BYOK) sends the transcript text to the provider you picked, such as OpenAI or Anthropic Claude. If you connect an AI assistant over MCP, it sends what it reads to its own AI provider. Integrations are off by default, and nothing is connected until you turn it on.
Can Meetily help with incident reviews and postmortems?
Yes. Record the incident review, summarize it with a custom summary template that has sections for timeline, root cause and follow-ups, and export the notes as Markdown for your postmortem doc. Speaker diarization shows who said what. Custom templates and speaker diarization are Pro features; the Community Edition gives you the local transcript and a standard AI summary.
Does Meetily run on Linux?
Meetily has native apps for Windows and macOS (Apple Silicon). There is no native Linux app. The open source code is on GitHub if you want to build it yourself.
What does Meetily Pro cost for an engineering team?
Pro is $10/user/month billed annually (early bird pricing; the regular price is $25/month), with a 14-day free trial that needs no payment. Pro adds speaker diarization, custom summary templates, PDF, DOCX and Markdown export, automatic recording, and the MCP server, webhooks, CLI and local API. The Community Edition is free and open source (MIT) and uses the same transcription engine as Pro. Enterprise, for self-hosted deployment and admin controls, has custom pricing.

AI Meeting Assistant FAQ
What is an AI meeting assistant?
An AI meeting assistant records and transcribes your calls, then helps with what comes after: a summary with decisions and action items, a record of past meetings you can search, and answers to questions like "what did we agree on last week?". Meetily transcribes on your own computer, and in Pro your AI assistant can work with your meeting history through MCP.
How is an AI meeting assistant different from an AI note taker?
A note taker writes up one meeting. An assistant also works across meetings: it finds the call where something was decided, lists open action items, and passes notes to the tools you already use. In Meetily, transcription and summaries are in every edition. Searching from an AI assistant or a script, and webhooks for automation, are Pro features.
Can I ask Claude about my past meetings?
Yes, with Meetily Pro. Pro includes an MCP server, and Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Windsurf connect in one click when they are installed on the same computer. The assistant can search your meetings and read transcripts and summaries. New connections start read-only, and starting or stopping a recording is a separate permission. Browser-based assistants such as Claude on the web cannot reach it. Keep in mind that an assistant sends what it reads to its own AI provider.
Does Meetily join my calls as a bot?
No. Meetily records the audio on your own computer, so no extra participant shows up in Zoom, Teams, Google Meet or any other app, and no host has to let anything in. As with any recorder, tell the people in the meeting that you are recording.
Can it send meeting notes to Slack, Notion or Jira?
Not with a built-in connector. Meetily Pro gives your own script a signed webhook when a summary is ready, and a command-line tool that exports any meeting as Markdown, text or JSON. The script then posts the notes wherever you want them. A connected AI assistant that already has access to those apps can do the same step for you.
Where are my meetings processed?
Recording and transcription (Whisper or Parakeet) run on your computer, and the audio is never uploaded. Summaries use a local model through Ollama or the built-in model, or your own API key for Claude, OpenAI or Groq. With an API key, the transcript text goes to that provider under its terms. Zackriya Solutions does not receive your recordings or transcripts.
Is there a free version?
Yes. The Community Edition is free and open source (MIT license): live transcription, AI summaries, audio import and retranscription, all on your computer. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually and adds speaker diarization, custom summary templates, meeting detection with automatic recording, PDF and DOCX export, and the MCP server, CLI and webhooks. Pro comes with a 14-day trial.
Which platforms and languages does it support?
Meetily runs on Windows and on macOS with Apple Silicon. There is no native Linux app. It transcribes 99+ languages and works with any meeting app that plays audio on your computer, including Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet and Webex.

AI Note Taker FAQ
What is the best AI note taker for meetings?
If privacy comes first for you, Meetily is a strong option. Otter.ai and Fireflies process meetings in the cloud; Meetily transcribes on your device, so your meeting audio is never uploaded. Summaries use a local model, or your own API key if you choose. It works with Zoom, Google Meet, Microsoft Teams, and any other meeting platform. Community Edition is free, and Pro is $10/user/month billed annually.
Is there a free AI note taker?
Yes, Meetily Community Edition is a free, open-source AI note taker with no usage caps. Many free tiers limit your minutes; Community Edition does not. It includes real-time transcription, AI-powered summaries, and action item extraction. It runs locally on your device with no account required.
How does AI note taking work?
AI note taking uses speech recognition to transcribe your meetings in real-time, then applies a language model to identify key points, action items, and generate summaries. Meetily transcribes on your device with Whisper or Parakeet. For summaries, you pick a local model (Ollama or the built-in model) or your own API key. With a local model, no internet connection is needed during or after meetings.
Can AI note takers join Zoom meetings?
Many AI note takers, such as Otter.ai and Fireflies, can join meetings as a bot participant, which may need host approval. Meetily works differently - it captures audio directly from your device without joining the call, so no bot appears in your meeting. Let participants know you are recording. This works with Zoom, Teams, Meet, and any other platform.
Is AI note taking private and secure?
With cloud-based AI note takers, your meeting audio is uploaded to external servers. Meetily is different - transcription happens on your device, and the audio is never uploaded. Summaries use a local model, or your own API key if you choose; with an API key, the transcript text goes to that provider. The Community Edition is open source, so you can check how it handles data yourself. That makes it a good fit for confidential discussions in healthcare, legal, and enterprise settings.
What is the difference between AI note taker and AI notetaker?
There is no difference - "AI note taker" and "AI notetaker" refer to the same thing. Both terms describe software that uses artificial intelligence to automatically capture and organize notes from meetings or lectures. Meetily is an AI note taker (or notetaker) that transcribes on your device.
Can I use an AI note taker for lectures and classes?
Yes, Meetily works great for lectures and classes. It can transcribe in real-time as your professor speaks, capture key concepts, and generate study notes. With a local summary model it works offline after initial setup, so you can use it even in locations with poor internet. The notes are stored locally on your device for easy review later.
Does the AI note taker work with Microsoft Teams?
Yes, Meetily works with Microsoft Teams, as well as Zoom, Google Meet, Webex, Discord, and any other meeting platform. Since it captures audio at the system level rather than integrating with specific platforms, it works universally. There is no plugin or meeting integration to set up - just start your meeting and Meetily handles the rest.

AI Transcription FAQ
What is the best AI transcription software?
For users who prioritize privacy, Meetily is a strong choice. It uses OpenAI's open-source Whisper model (or NVIDIA Parakeet) and runs it on your local device. Cloud services like Otter.ai or Rev process audio on their servers; with Meetily, your audio never leaves your computer. It supports 99+ languages, real-time transcription, and works offline. Community Edition is free.
Is there free AI transcription software?
Yes, Meetily Community Edition is free AI transcription software with no usage limits. Cloud-based alternatives often limit free users to a few hours per month; Community Edition does not. It runs locally on your device, requires no account, and includes AI summaries. Speaker diarization (who said what) is part of Meetily Pro.
How accurate is AI transcription?
Meetily uses Whisper AI, which delivers high accuracy for most languages and accents. Since processing happens locally, you can use higher-quality models without worrying about upload speeds. Clear audio with minimal background noise produces the best results.
Can AI transcription work offline?
Yes, Meetily works completely offline after initial installation. The AI models run locally on your device, so no internet connection is required during transcription. This makes it a good fit for sensitive environments, locations with poor connectivity, or air-gapped systems. Once the model is downloaded, transcription needs no connection.
Is AI transcription private and secure?
With cloud-based AI transcription, your audio is uploaded to external servers for processing, creating privacy risks. Meetily is different - all transcription happens locally on your device, and your audio is never uploaded. Summaries are your choice: a local model keeps the transcript on your computer, while your own API key sends the transcript text to that provider. The Community Edition is open source, so you can check how it handles data yourself.
What languages does AI transcription support?
Meetily supports transcription in over 99 languages through Whisper AI. This includes major languages like English, Spanish, French, German, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, Arabic, Hindi, and Portuguese, plus many regional languages. It can also detect languages automatically and handle multilingual content.
Can I transcribe audio files with AI?
Yes, Meetily can transcribe pre-recorded audio files in addition to live audio. Simply import your MP3, WAV, M4A, or other audio files and Meetily will transcribe them locally. This is useful for podcasts, recorded interviews, lectures, or any audio content you need converted to text.
How does AI transcription compare to human transcription?
AI transcription with Meetily is much faster and cheaper than human transcription. A human transcriptionist usually needs several hours per hour of audio and charges by the minute, while Meetily transcribes in real time, and the Community Edition is free. Accuracy is high for clear audio. Human transcription may still be preferred for heavily accented speech or poor audio quality.

Voice Recorder FAQ
What is a voice recorder with transcription?
A voice recorder with transcription combines audio recording and automatic speech-to-text conversion in one tool. Instead of recording first and transcribing later with a separate service, Meetily does both simultaneously - you record and get text in real-time.
How does Meetily compare to dedicated voice recorder apps?
Basic voice recorders only capture audio. Meetily also transcribes the recording and writes a summary with action items. Community Edition is free and open source, and Pro adds features like PDF/DOCX export and speaker diarization.
Can I record system audio like meeting calls?
Yes! Meetily can capture system audio from any application - Zoom, Teams, Google Meet, browser videos, or any audio playing on your computer. That means you can record online meetings without a bot joining the call.
Is the transcription accurate?
Meetily offers two engines: Whisper when accuracy matters most, or Parakeet for speed. Both give high accuracy on clear audio. Results vary with audio quality, background noise, and accents.
Does my recording get uploaded anywhere?
No. Recording and transcription happen on your device, and your audio files are never uploaded. Summaries use a local model, or your own API key if you choose, in which case the transcript text goes to that provider.
Is it really free?

HIPAA Technical Safeguards

How Meetily's design relates to the Security Rule's technical safeguards. Your organization still implements and documents them.

HIPAA Requirement	Meetily	Cloud Services
Access Controls (164.312(a))	Your device's own access controls	Shared vendor infrastructure
Transmission Security (164.312(e))	Audio is not transmitted for transcription	Encrypted upload to the vendor
Integrity Controls (164.312(c))	Local storage you manage	Vendor cloud storage
Audit Controls (164.312(b))	Open-source Community Edition code	Vendor-controlled
Summary processing	Local model, or your own API key	Vendor's AI service
