HIPAA and Meetily FAQ
Does using Meetily make my practice HIPAA compliant?
No software makes an organization HIPAA compliant on its own, and HIPAA does not certify products. Compliance is a program your organization runs. Meetily is designed for HIPAA-aligned workflows: recording and transcription run on your device, so patient audio is never uploaded, and there is no Meetily cloud storing your transcripts. A local summary model keeps the transcript on the device too; with your own cloud API key, that provider receives the transcript text.
Can I use an AI meeting assistant for medical meetings?
Yes, with the right setup. Meetily can transcribe and summarize healthcare discussions on the clinician's computer when you pair local transcription with a local summary model. Cloud transcription services receive the audio, which is why they are usually reviewed as business associates. With Meetily, recordings and transcripts stay on your device and are not sent to Zackriya Solutions. Whether that changes your obligations is a call for your compliance or legal team.
Can I use AI meeting notes in healthcare without violating HIPAA?
It depends on how the tool handles PHI and how your organization uses it. Tools that send audio to external servers add another party to the flow of PHI. Meetily keeps recording and transcription on the device, and summaries can run on a local model, so PHI does not have to leave your own systems. Meetily does not replace your policies on access, retention and patient consent, and your organization stays responsible for its own compliance program.
Do I need a BAA (Business Associate Agreement) with Meetily?
That is a determination for your compliance or legal team, and we cannot make it for you. What we can describe is the data flow: Meetily records and transcribes on your device, recordings and transcripts are stored there, and Zackriya Solutions does not receive them. If you use a cloud AI provider for summaries with your own API key, that provider receives the transcript text, so check whether you need a BAA with that provider.
How does Meetily protect PHI during telehealth sessions?
Meetily captures the call audio and transcribes it with on-device models, so the audio of a virtual visit is never uploaded. The transcript is stored locally. Nothing is sent to an external server unless you choose a cloud AI provider for summaries, in which case that provider receives the transcript text. Pick a local summary model to keep the whole session on the device.
Is Meetily suitable for mental health and therapy sessions?
It can be, where confidentiality comes first. Recording and transcription happen on the therapist's computer, so session audio is not transmitted to or stored on external servers. For the strictest setups, use a local summary model so the transcript stays on the device too. Get the patient's consent before recording, and follow your own documentation policies.
Can hospital IT review how Meetily handles data?
Yes. The Community Edition is open source (MIT license), so your IT security team can read the code to see how audio is captured, transcribed and stored, and confirm that transcription runs locally. Meetily Pro adds features on top of the same on-device transcription. Your team can then judge whether your setup meets your technical safeguard requirements.
How do I handle data retention and destruction with Meetily?
Recordings and transcripts are stored on your device, and you delete meetings in the app or remove the recording files. There is no Meetily cloud copy to request deletion from. If you use a cloud AI provider for summaries, check its retention terms for the transcript text it receives.

What is Otter.ai?

Otter.ai is a cloud-based meeting transcription and note-taking service from AI Sense Inc., founded in 2016 by Sam Liang and Yun Fu. It joins meetings on Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams as the OtterPilot bot, captures real-time transcripts with speaker labels, and generates AI summaries and action items. It targets sales, recruiting, education, and general business users, and operates on a freemium SaaS model with paid plans roughly in the $8.33-$30/user/month range billed annually.

Meetily takes a different architectural approach to the same workflow. Rather than sending audio to a cloud service, Meetily Community runs Whisper-based transcription locally on Windows or macOS, so audio and transcripts stay on your device. Summaries are pluggable - you can keep them local or bring your own API key. Community is free and open source under MIT; Pro is $10/user/month billed annually for premium support. If you'd like to try the local-first model, download Meetily at /downloads.

What is Otter.ai?
Otter.ai is a cloud-based meeting transcription and note-taking service from AI Sense Inc., founded in 2016 by Sam Liang and Yun Fu. It joins meetings on Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams as the OtterPilot bot, captures real-time transcripts with speaker labels, and generates AI summaries and action items. It targets sales, recruiting, education, and general business users, and operates on a freemium SaaS model with paid plans roughly in the $8.33-$30/user/month range billed annually. Meetily takes a different architectural approach to the same workflow. Rather than sending audio to a cloud service, Meetily Community runs Whisper-based transcription locally on Windows or macOS, so audio and transcripts stay on your device. What is Meetily and how is it different from Otter.ai?
Meetily is an open source AI meeting assistant that processes everything locally on your device. Unlike Otter.ai which stores data in the cloud, Meetily ensures 100% privacy with no external data transmission. Key differences: 100% Local Processing (vs cloud-based), Free (vs $8-30/month), Open Source (vs proprietary), GDPR/HIPAA Compliant by Design (vs limited compliance).
Is Meetily really free compared to Otter.ai?

Enterprise deployments have custom pricing. Otter.ai charges $8.33-$30/month per user for everyone. Community users save $96-360+ per year with Meetily.
Which is better for privacy: Meetily or Otter.ai?
Meetily is significantly better for privacy. All data processing happens locally on your device with no external transmission. Otter.ai processes and stores all your meeting data in the cloud. For GDPR, HIPAA, or any privacy-sensitive use case, Meetily provides complete data sovereignty while Otter.ai requires trusting third-party cloud processing.
Can Meetily transcribe as accurately as Otter.ai?
Yes, Meetily uses state-of-the-art speech recognition models (Whisper) that provide accuracy comparable to or better than Otter.ai, especially for technical discussions and specialized terminology. The advantage is that all processing happens locally, so your data never leaves your control.
Does Meetily work offline like Otter.ai requires internet?
Yes, Meetily can work completely offline since all processing happens locally on your device. Otter.ai requires constant internet connectivity as it sends audio to cloud servers for processing. This makes Meetily ideal for secure environments or situations with limited connectivity.
How does cost compare over time between Meetily and Otter.ai?
Over 5 years: Meetily Community edition costs $0 (free), Otter.ai Pro costs $500 ($8.33/month), Otter.ai Business costs $1,200 ($20/month), and Otter.ai Enterprise costs $1,800 ($30/month). Community users save $500-1,800 over 5 years. Meetily Pro is $10/user/month billed annually ($120/year).

Is Meetily suitable for enterprise teams like Otter.ai?

Bot-Free Recording: Common Questions
What is Meetily and how is it different from Fireflies.ai?
Meetily is a bot-free AI meeting assistant that processes everything locally on your device. Unlike Fireflies.ai which requires a meeting bot to join every call, Meetily captures audio directly from your computer without any visible presence in meetings. Key differences: No Meeting Bot (vs bot required), 100% Local Processing (vs cloud-based), Free (vs $10-40/month), Complete Privacy (vs third-party processing).
Why don't meeting participants see Meetily like they see Fireflies bots?
Meetily captures audio directly from your device's system audio, just like screen recording software. No bot joins the meeting, so there's no 'Fireflies Notetaker' or similar presence visible to other participants. This makes recording completely private and non-disruptive, while still capturing everything for transcription.
Is bot-free recording better than using meeting bots?
Bot-free recording offers several advantages: complete privacy (no one knows you're recording), works with any meeting platform, no permission issues, no disruption to meeting flow, and better compliance for sensitive discussions. Meeting bots require participant awareness, can create legal/privacy concerns, and may change meeting dynamics.
Can Meetily work with any meeting platform like Fireflies requires integrations?
Yes, Meetily works with ANY meeting platform since it captures system audio directly - Zoom, Teams, Google Meet, WebEx, Slack calls, or even phone calls. Fireflies requires specific integrations and platform permissions, limiting where it can work and requiring setup for each platform.
How much money can I save by switching from Fireflies to Meetily?
Meetily Community edition is completely free (open source). Fireflies costs $10-40/month ($120-480/year). Community users save $600-2,400 over 5 years. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually ($120/year) for enhanced features. Enterprise has custom pricing.
Is Meetily suitable for confidential meetings where bot presence is inappropriate?
Yes, Meetily is perfect for confidential meetings. Since no bot joins the call, you can record sensitive discussions (legal consultations, medical appointments, executive meetings, HR discussions) without alerting other participants or creating awkward disclosure requirements that visible bots necessitate.
Does Meetily require permissions from meeting hosts like Fireflies does?
No, Meetily doesn't require meeting host permissions. Since it captures audio from your device (like taking notes yourself), it works regardless of meeting platform settings or host restrictions. Fireflies bots can be blocked by meeting hosts who don't allow bots.
Can I migrate my data from Fireflies to Meetily?
Yes, you can export your existing meeting transcripts from Fireflies before switching. While the formats differ, you can keep your historical data for reference. Going forward, all new meetings will be processed locally with Meetily, giving you complete control and privacy.

Privacy-First vs Cloud Sync: The Critical Difference

Why local processing and data sovereignty matter for modern teams

Complete Data Sovereignty

Meetily: Transcription and storage stay on your device by default, and summaries run on a local model or your own API key. Granola: Audio goes to third-party transcription providers and notes are stored on AWS in the US.

Pricing, Like for Like

Meetily: Community Edition free (open source); Pro $10/user/month billed annually; Enterprise custom. Granola: Basic $0 with 30 days of history; Business $14 and Enterprise $35 per user per month.

Open Source vs Proprietary

Meetily: MIT licensed, full source code access, community-driven. Granola: Closed source, limited customization, vendor lock-in.

100% Local Processing

Meetily: Transcription runs locally by default, and summaries can run on a local model. Granola: Audio is transcribed by third-party cloud providers, and summaries run on OpenAI and Anthropic.

Team Consistency

Meetily: Same privacy-first tool for everyone on Windows and macOS, with unified workflows. Open source transparency means your team can audit the code.

Deployment Flexibility

Meetily: Native macOS and Windows apps today; Linux build-from-source available now via the open-source repo, native Linux desktop app on the waitlist (/granola-for-linux). Granola: Vendor-managed cloud distribution, no Linux desktop app.

Cross-Platform Support: Common Questions
What is Meetily and how is it different from Granola?
Meetily is an open source (MIT) AI meeting assistant. Transcription runs on your device by default, and recordings and notes are stored on your machine. For summaries you choose a local model or your own API key. Granola is closed source: it sends audio to third-party transcription providers, deletes the recording once the transcript exists, and stores notes on AWS in the US. Both run on Windows and macOS, and neither sends a bot into your call. Meetily Community Edition is free; Granola Basic is $0, with Business at $14 and Enterprise at $35 per user per month.
Does Granola work on Windows like Meetily?
Yes. Granola supports Windows and macOS, as well as iPhone and Android. The difference is where your meeting data goes. Granola sends audio to third-party transcription providers and stores notes in its cloud. Meetily transcribes on your device by default, and on that path your audio stays on your machine. For summaries you choose a local model or your own API key, in both Community and Pro.
Why is cross-platform support important for meeting tools?
Cross-platform support ensures all team members can use the same tool regardless of their operating system, maintaining consistency in meeting workflows, file formats, and features. It eliminates the need for multiple tool licenses and training, reduces IT complexity, and ensures better collaboration across diverse technology environments.
Can I self-host Meetily for my organization?
Yes. Meetily ships native desktop apps for macOS (.dmg) and Windows (EXE). For Linux desktop today, build the app bundle from source via the open-source repo (github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily); a native Linux desktop app is in development - join the Granola for Linux waitlist (/granola-for-linux). For Enterprise self-hosted deployments, contact meetily@zackriya.com.
How much can I save by switching from Granola to Meetily?
Both have a free plan. Meetily Community Edition is free and open source (MIT). Granola Basic is $0 per user per month, with 30 days of note history in the app. On paid plans, Meetily Pro is $10/user/month billed annually ($120/year) and Granola Business is $14/user/month ($168/year). Granola Enterprise is $35/user/month; Meetily Enterprise has custom pricing.
Is Meetily's open source model better than Granola's proprietary approach?
Meetily's open source model (MIT license) offers transparency, customization, no vendor lock-in, and community-driven improvements. You can inspect the code, modify functionality, and contribute features. Granola's proprietary approach limits you to their roadmap and doesn't allow customization or code inspection.
Does Meetily process data locally like Granola claims to?
Yes. Transcription always runs on your device, in every edition, and Community Edition is local end to end. Granola works differently: its security page says audio is sent to third-party transcription providers such as Deepgram and Assembly, and notes are stored on AWS in the US. For summaries on Meetily, you choose: local AI or your own API key (BYOK). With a local model, nothing leaves your machine.
Can teams with mixed Windows/Mac environments use the same tool?
Yes, with Meetily. It supports Windows and macOS, so all team members use the same tool with identical features and workflows. Both Meetily and Granola support Windows and macOS. Meetily's difference is that transcription and storage stay on your own machine by default, with no cloud sync.

Meetily vs Read AI: Common Questions
What is Meetily and how is it different from Read AI?
Meetily is an open source AI meeting assistant that processes everything locally on your device. Read AI is a cloud-based service that uses bots to join meetings and processes data on their servers. Key differences: Meetily has 100% local processing (vs cloud), no meeting bot (vs visible bot joining calls), open source code (vs proprietary), and a free Community edition (vs 5 meetings/month limit on Read AI free tier).
How does Read AI pricing compare to Meetily?
Read AI Free: 5 meetings/month. Read AI Pro: $15-19.75/month. Read AI Enterprise: $22.50-29.75/month. Enterprise+ (with HIPAA): $29.75-39.75/month. Meetily Community: Free with unlimited meetings. Meetily Pro: $10/user/month (billed annually at $120). For teams of 5+, Meetily saves $300-900+ per year compared to Read AI.
Does Read AI use a meeting bot?
Yes, Read AI uses a bot that joins your meetings as a visible participant. This can be awkward, may require host approval, and alerts other participants that the meeting is being recorded. Meetily captures system audio directly without any bot - only you know you're transcribing.
Which is better for privacy: Meetily or Read AI?
Meetily is significantly better for privacy. All processing happens locally on your device with zero external transmission. Read AI processes audio on their cloud servers and stores your meeting data externally. Read AI is SOC 2 and GDPR compliant, but HIPAA compliance requires their most expensive Enterprise+ plan ($29.75-39.75/month). Meetily is GDPR/HIPAA compliant by design since no data leaves your device.
What integrations does Read AI have that Meetily doesn't?
Read AI offers premium integrations with Notion, Salesforce, HubSpot, Jira, Confluence, Slack, and Zapier (available on Pro plan). Meetily focuses on local-first functionality with export options (PDF, DOCX in Pro). Meetily Pro also lets your AI assistant (via MCP) and your own scripts (signed webhooks and a built-in command-line tool) work with your meetings, but it has no native one-click connectors. If you need CRM integrations without scripting, Read AI has more options, but at the cost of your data being processed in the cloud.
Can Meetily transcribe as accurately as Read AI?
Yes, Meetily uses state-of-the-art Whisper models that provide accuracy comparable to Read AI. Both support 20+ languages. The advantage of Meetily is that processing happens locally, so your sensitive conversations never leave your device.
Does Meetily work offline unlike Read AI?
Yes, Meetily works completely offline since all processing happens locally. Read AI requires constant internet connectivity as it streams audio to cloud servers for processing. This makes Meetily ideal for secure environments, travel, or areas with limited connectivity.
What are Read AI's main drawbacks?
Based on user feedback: 1) Limited free plan (only 5 meetings/month), 2) Visible bot joining meetings can be awkward, 3) Expensive for teams ($15-40/user/month), 4) Cloud processing means your data is on their servers, 5) HIPAA compliance only on most expensive tier. Meetily addresses all of these with local processing, no bot, and a generous free tier.

Meetily vs Zoom AI: Frequently Asked Questions
What is Meetily and how is it different from Zoom AI Companion?
Meetily is a privacy-first AI meeting assistant that works with ANY meeting platform and processes everything locally. Unlike Zoom AI Companion which only works with Zoom and sends data to the cloud, Meetily ensures complete privacy with universal platform support. Key differences: Works with All Platforms (vs Zoom-only), 100% Local Processing (vs cloud-based), Free Community Edition (vs $240/year), Platform Independence (vs vendor lock-in).
Can Meetily work with Zoom AND other platforms unlike Zoom AI?
Yes! Meetily works with Zoom, Teams, Google Meet, Slack, Discord, WebEx, and any other video conferencing platform. Zoom AI Companion ONLY works with Zoom meetings, forcing you to use different tools for non-Zoom meetings. Meetily gives you one tool for all platforms.
Is Meetily really free compared to Zoom AI's $240/year cost?
Yes, Meetily Community edition is completely free and open source with no subscription fees. Zoom AI Companion costs $20/month ($240/year) and requires a Zoom Pro subscription ($15.99/month additional). Community users save $240+ per year. Pro edition at $10/user/month billed annually ($120/year) for enhanced features.
Which is better for privacy: Meetily or Zoom AI Companion?
Meetily is significantly better for privacy. All transcription happens locally on your device with zero cloud transmission. Zoom AI processes all meeting data in Zoom's cloud servers. For GDPR, HIPAA, or privacy-sensitive environments, Meetily provides complete data sovereignty while Zoom AI requires trusting Zoom's cloud infrastructure.
What if I use Zoom for some meetings and Teams for others?
Meetily is perfect for this scenario - it works seamlessly with both Zoom and Teams (and all other platforms). With Zoom AI, you'd need a completely different solution for Teams meetings, creating workflow complexity and additional costs. Meetily provides consistent functionality across all platforms.
Does Meetily work offline unlike Zoom AI?
Yes, Meetily works completely offline since all processing happens locally. Zoom AI Companion requires constant internet connectivity to Zoom's cloud servers. This makes Meetily ideal for secure environments, air-gapped networks, or situations with unreliable connectivity.
Can I migrate away from Zoom AI to Meetily?
Yes, you can export your meeting data from Zoom before switching to Meetily. Going forward, all new meetings across ALL platforms (not just Zoom) will be processed locally with Meetily, giving you platform independence and complete privacy.
How does cost compare over 5 years?
Over 5 years: Meetily Community costs $0 (free), Zoom AI Companion costs $1,200 ($20/month x 60 months) PLUS Zoom Pro subscription costs ($960+). Total Zoom cost: $2,160+. Community users save $2,160+ over 5 years. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually ($120/year).

ChatGPT Record Mode vs Meetily: Local-First Meeting Recording (No Cloud, No Account)

What ChatGPT Record Mode Does Well, and Where Meetily Differs

Both tools transcribe and summarize meetings. The honest difference is where your data lives and what you have to pay (and sign up) for to get there.

ChatGPT Record Mode: Great if you already pay for ChatGPT

If you already have ChatGPT Plus / Team and you're fine with OpenAI processing your audio, Record Mode is genuinely convenient - one app, one subscription, summaries live in your chat history. The tradeoff is that every recording goes to OpenAI.

Meetily: Audio never leaves your device

Meetily transcribes locally on Windows and macOS using on-device Whisper or Parakeet models. With the Community Edition, nothing leaves your machine - no audio, no transcript, no account. Pricing: free Community Edition, or $10/user/month billed annually for Pro.

You choose the summary model

ChatGPT Record Mode is tied to OpenAI models. Meetily lets you pick: local Ollama / built-in model for full privacy or bring-your-own key (Claude, OpenAI, Groq). The model that suits your privacy and quality needs - not the vendor's.

Works offline

ChatGPT Record Mode needs an internet connection to upload audio. Meetily Community runs entirely offline once installed, so it works on flights, in secure rooms, and on flaky hotel wifi.

99+ languages, on device

Meetily ships with local Whisper / Parakeet models that handle 99+ languages without sending audio off-device. ChatGPT Record Mode also supports broad language coverage, but transcription happens in the cloud.

No account, no lock-in

You can install Meetily, record a meeting, and never create an account. ChatGPT Record Mode requires an active ChatGPT Plus subscription tied to your OpenAI account. If you cancel, you lose access to the recording flow.
How to Use Meetily as a Local-First Alternative

Keep your ChatGPT subscription if you want - Meetily slots in next to it for the recording itself.

1. Install Meetily (free Community Edition)

Download Meetily for Windows or macOS. No account, no signup. The Community Edition is free and transcribes 100% locally on your machine.

2. Record meetings on device

Meetily captures system audio directly, with no meeting bot. Audio and the resulting transcript stay on your machine. Use any meeting platform - Zoom, Meet, Teams, WebEx.

3. Summarize how you want

Pick a summary path: local Ollama / built-in model (nothing leaves) or bring-your-own key (including OpenAI / ChatGPT). With BYOK, only transcript text is sent off-device - never audio.
ChatGPT Record Mode vs Meetily: FAQ
Does ChatGPT record meetings natively?
Yes. OpenAI's ChatGPT Record Mode (rolled out to Plus, Pro, Team, and Enterprise users) lets you start a recording inside the ChatGPT app and have it transcribed and summarized by ChatGPT. The audio is sent to OpenAI's servers for transcription and summarization. It works as a built-in feature of ChatGPT, not as a separate desktop recorder.
Where does ChatGPT Record Mode store my audio?
ChatGPT Record Mode uploads your audio to OpenAI's servers for processing. The resulting transcript and summary are saved to your ChatGPT chat history, which lives in OpenAI's cloud and is tied to your account. The audio is processed off-device, so the raw recording leaves your machine.
Do I need a ChatGPT account to use Record Mode?
Yes. ChatGPT Record Mode requires a signed-in ChatGPT account on a paid plan (Plus is $20/month, with Team and Enterprise on different rates). Without an account and an active subscription, you can't access the feature.
Is the transcript shared with OpenAI?
Yes - the transcript and summary are generated by OpenAI's models and stored in your ChatGPT history on OpenAI's infrastructure. Whether the data is used to improve OpenAI models depends on your account-level data controls (consumer vs Team/Enterprise plans have different defaults). If you want the transcript and audio to stay on your device, you need a local-first tool, not a cloud one.
Can I use ChatGPT Record Mode offline?
No. ChatGPT Record Mode requires an internet connection because the audio is uploaded to OpenAI for transcription and summarization. If your connection drops or you're working in a no-network environment, the feature won't work.
What's a private alternative to ChatGPT Record Mode?
Meetily is a local-first alternative. The Community Edition runs transcription entirely on your device using local Whisper or Parakeet models, with no account, no cloud upload, and no subscription. Meetily Pro lets you bring your own API key for summaries, but audio always stays local - only transcript text is ever sent off-device, and only if you choose to use an external provider. Meetily supports 99+ languages on Windows and macOS.
Does Meetily require an OpenAI key?
No. Meetily Community runs fully on-device with no keys at all. If you want to summarize with an external model you can bring your own API key (Claude, OpenAI, Groq), but neither is required. The default path is 100% local.
Can Meetily summarize with ChatGPT after the fact?
Yes. Meetily separates recording from summarization on purpose. You record locally with Meetily, then choose how to summarize: local AI (Ollama or the built-in model) or bring-your-own key (including OpenAI / ChatGPT). So you can still get a ChatGPT-quality summary without sending the audio itself to OpenAI - only the transcript text.

How to Write Meeting Minutes

Follow these steps for professional meeting documentation

1
Prepare Before the Meeting

Review the agenda and previous meeting minutes. Set up your template with basic details like date, time, and expected attendees. Have the agenda ready to guide your notes.

2
Capture Key Information During the Meeting

Record who attended, key discussion points for each agenda item, all decisions made, and action items with assigned owners and deadlines. Focus on outcomes, not every detail.

3
Format and Distribute Promptly

Clean up your notes within 24-48 hours while the meeting is fresh. Use a consistent format, check for accuracy, and distribute to all attendees and stakeholders.

Pro Tip: Skip manual note-taking entirely. Meetily automatically transcribes and summarizes your meetings with AI.

Try automatic meeting notes →
Meeting Templates FAQ
What is a meeting minutes template?
A meeting minutes template is a structured document format for recording what happened during a meeting - including attendees, discussion points, decisions made, and action items. Templates help ensure consistency and make it easy to reference past meetings.
What's the difference between meeting minutes and meeting notes?
Meeting minutes are formal, detailed records that document decisions, votes, and action items. They're often required for legal or compliance purposes. Meeting notes are less formal summaries of key discussion points and takeaways for quick reference.
How do I write effective meeting minutes?
Start with meeting details (date, attendees, purpose). List agenda items and capture key discussion points for each. Record all decisions and action items with assigned owners and deadlines. Keep it objective - document what was said, not your interpretation.
Can Meetily auto-generate meeting notes?
Yes! Meetily can automatically transcribe your meetings and generate AI summaries with key points, decisions, and action items. Download Meetily to stop taking manual notes - get instant, accurate meeting documentation.
What should be included in meeting minutes?
Essential elements: Meeting date, time, and location; attendees and absentees; agenda items discussed; key points from discussions; decisions made; action items with owners and deadlines; date of next meeting. For formal meetings, also include motions, seconders, and votes.
How long after a meeting should minutes be distributed?
Best practice is within 24-48 hours while the meeting is fresh. For formal board meetings, minutes are often distributed within a week and approved at the next meeting. With Meetily, you can share AI-generated notes immediately after the meeting.

Why Meetily is GDPR Compliant by Design

Local processing architecture automatically satisfies most GDPR requirements

Local Processing

All meeting transcription and AI processing happens locally on your devices. No meeting data is transmitted to external servers, ensuring complete data control.

Data Sovereignty

Meeting data remains within your organization's jurisdiction at all times, automatically satisfying GDPR's territorial requirements.

Privacy by Design

Meetily implements privacy by design principles (Article 25), with no data collection infrastructure or cloud dependencies.

No DPA Required

Since no data is processed by third parties, Data Processing Agreements (DPAs) with external vendors are not required for core functionality.

Data Minimization

Only processes audio data necessary for transcription. No excessive data collection, meeting GDPR's data minimization requirements.

User Rights Support

Easy compliance with data subject rights: access, portability, erasure, and modification all under your control.
GDPR Principles & Meetily Alignment

How Meetily satisfies key GDPR requirements

Article 5: Principles of Processing
✅ Lawfulness, fairness, transparency

Local processing with user consent ensures lawful basis

✅ Purpose limitation

Data used only for meeting transcription and summaries

✅ Data minimization

Only processes audio data necessary for transcription

✅ Storage limitation

User controls data retention on their local devices

Article 25: Data Protection by Design
Technical measures: Local processing eliminates data transfer risks
Organizational measures: Privacy-first architecture by default
Data minimization: Only processes necessary meeting audio
Pseudonymization: Meeting participants can be anonymized
Article 32: Security of Processing
🔐 Encryption

Local file encryption at rest, secure processing

🛡️ Confidentiality

No network transmission maintains confidentiality

🔄 Integrity

Local processing prevents unauthorized alterations

⚡ Availability

Offline capability ensures continuous availability
Country-Specific GDPR Implementation

Meetily helps you comply with national GDPR implementations across the EU

🇩🇪 Germany (DSGVO + BDSG)

100% German data residency, no US data transfers eliminating Schrems II concerns, native German transcription support, and compliance with Bundesdatenschutzgesetz.

🇫🇷 France (RGPD + CNIL)

CNIL-compliant by design with local processing, French cloud deployment options (OVH), high-accuracy French transcription for business and legal sectors.

🇪🇸 Spain (LOPDGDD)

LOPDGDD Article 89 digital disconnection compliance, local control for access restrictions, AEPD-compliant architecture for strict enforcement requirements.

🇳🇱 Netherlands (UAVG)

Complete transparency for Autoriteit Persoonsgegevens requirements, local data inspection capability, automated decision-making oversight support.

🇮🇹 Italy (Privacy Code)

Garante guidelines compliance for workplace recording, worker council consultation support, local processing for full organizational control.

All 27 EU Member States

Automatic compliance with EU-wide GDPR requirements, regional deployment flexibility, multi-language transcription support across European languages.

🇪🇺 European Healthcare Organizations

If you're a healthcare provider in the EU, GDPR compliance is more relevant than HIPAA.HIPAA applies to US healthcare organizations, while EU healthcare must comply with GDPR Article 9 (special category data) and country-specific laws like DSGVO (Germany), RGPD (France), or national healthcare data protection regulations.

Why Meetily Works for HIPAA

Local processing architecture eliminates PHI transmission risks

Local PHI Processing

All meeting transcription happens locally on your devices. PHI never leaves your healthcare network, eliminating transmission risks and third-party exposure.

No Third-Party BAAs

Since no data is processed by third parties, Business Associate Agreements (BAAs) with external vendors are not required for core functionality.

Access Control Ready

You maintain complete control over who accesses meeting transcripts. Implement role-based access and audit trails as required by HIPAA.

Audit Trail Support

Local logging of all data processing activities. Track PHI access, modifications, and deletions for compliance documentation.

Minimum Necessary

Process only required meeting audio for transcription. Built-in data minimization satisfies HIPAA's minimum necessary standard.

Healthcare Optimized

Designed for patient consultations, medical team rounds, and research meetings while keeping PHI secure and local.
HIPAA Safeguards Implementation

How Meetily supports HIPAA Security Rule requirements

Technical Safeguards (§ 164.312)
🔐 Access Control
• Unique user authentication for device access
• Role-based access to meeting transcripts
• Automatic workstation locking
• Session timeout mechanisms
📊 Audit Controls
• User access logging to PHI transcripts
• Meeting recording timestamps
• Transcript generation and access tracking
• File modification and deletion logs
🔧 Data Integrity
• File system permissions (read-only after creation)
• Digital signatures for transcript files
• Version control for document changes
• Regular backup validation
✅ Transmission Security

Automatic Compliance: Meetily's local processing eliminates transmission security concerns - PHI is never transmitted over networks.

Administrative Safeguards (§ 164.308)
👤 Security Officer
• Oversee Meetily deployment security
• Monitor access to meeting transcripts
• Conduct regular security assessments
• Manage incident response procedures
🎓 Workforce Training
• HIPAA Privacy and Security Rules overview
• PHI identification in meeting contexts
• Meetily proper usage procedures
• Incident reporting requirements
⚠️ Contingency Plan
• Regular encrypted backups of transcripts
• Secure backup storage locations
• Disaster recovery procedures
• Business continuity planning
📋 Risk Assessment
• Conduct HIPAA Security Risk Assessment
• Document Meetily usage scenarios
• Identify and mitigate risks
• Regular compliance reviews
Physical Safeguards (§ 164.310)
🏢 Facility Access
• Secured physical locations
• Access card controls
• Visitor escort policies
• Security camera coverage
💻 Workstation Security
• Laptop encryption requirements
• Cable locks for desktops
• Secure storage when not in use
• Mobile device management
💾 Media Controls
• Secure disposal of storage media
• Backup tape security
• USB device restrictions
• Data sanitization procedures

1. Risk Assessment

2. Department Rollout

3. Organization-Wide

Understanding Data Sovereignty

What it means and why it matters for your organization

What is Data Sovereignty?

Data sovereignty is the concept that digital data is subject to the laws and governance structures of the nation or region where it is physically located or processed.

Location Control: Data remains within specified geographic boundaries
Legal Jurisdiction: Subject only to local laws and regulations
Access Control: Organizations maintain full control over data access
Processing Control: All processing happens within defined boundaries
Why It Matters
Regulatory Compliance

Meet GDPR, data localization laws, and industry-specific requirements

Security & Privacy

Reduce exposure to foreign surveillance and data breaches

Business Control

Maintain complete control over sensitive business information
How Meetily Ensures Data Sovereignty

Technical and organizational measures for complete data control

100% Local Processing

All AI processing runs locally on user devices. No internet connection required for core functionality, and meeting data never leaves organizational boundaries.

Air-Gapped Deployment

Deploy in completely isolated networks with no external connectivity. Perfect for classified or highly secure environments with maximum security posture.

Geographic Data Control

Deploy within specific countries or regions to meet local data residency laws. Complete control over where data is processed and stored.

No Foreign Access

Data remains within your chosen jurisdiction. No foreign government access risks, complete organizational control, and automatic compliance with local laws.

Zero Cloud Dependencies

Works completely offline with no cloud dependencies for transcription. All processing happens on your infrastructure under your control.

Data Portability

Export data in standard formats, move data between systems with no vendor lock-in, and maintain complete ownership of your information.
Global Data Sovereignty Requirements

How Meetily helps you comply with regional data protection laws

🇪🇺 European Union (GDPR)

Strict data protection rules requiring explicit consent for cross-border transfers

• Data must remain in EU/EEA or adequacy countries
• Standard Contractual Clauses required for third countries
• Right to data portability and erasure
• Meetily eliminates cross-border transfer risks entirely
🇨🇳 China (Cybersecurity Law)

Critical information infrastructure operators must store data locally

• Personal information must remain in China
• Security assessments required for data transfers
• Government approval needed for certain exports
• Meetily's local processing satisfies all requirements
🇷🇺 Russia (Data Localization)

Personal data of Russian citizens must be stored on servers in Russia

• Database localization requirements
• Registration with Roskomnadzor
• Strict penalties for non-compliance
• Local deployment ensures automatic compliance
🇮🇳 India (Data Protection Bill)

Proposed legislation requiring local processing of sensitive personal data

• Sensitive data must be processed in India
• Mirror copy requirements for certain data
• Restrictions on cross-border transfers
• Meetily architecture ready for compliance
🇦🇺 Australia (Privacy Act)

Enhanced privacy protections with notification requirements

• Notifiable data breaches scheme
• Cross-border disclosure restrictions
• Government data sovereignty initiatives
• Local processing minimizes breach risks
🇧🇷 Brazil (LGPD)

Comprehensive data protection law similar to GDPR

• Consent requirements for processing
• Data subject rights protection
• Transfer restrictions to third countries
• Local processing ensures compliance
Industry-Specific Data Sovereignty

How different sectors benefit from complete data control

Government & Public Sector

Meet classified information handling requirements and national security protocols. Perfect for government security clearance environments and public sector transparency.

Financial Services

Protect sensitive financial data while meeting banking and securities regulations. Support for banking secrecy compliance and regulatory examination requirements.

Healthcare

Maintain patient data within required jurisdictions while ensuring HIPAA/GDPR compliance. Support for medical research data sovereignty and cross-border healthcare.

Legal & Law Firms

Protect attorney-client privilege across international jurisdictions. Support for cross-border legal representation and international arbitration.

Multinational Corporations

Navigate complex multi-jurisdiction data protection requirements. Support for multi-region compliance and global privacy policy alignment.

Critical Infrastructure

Protect critical infrastructure communications and operational data. Support for energy sector security and national security protocols.
Data Sovereignty Implementation

Four steps to achieve complete data sovereignty

1. Assess Requirements

Evaluate your organization's data sovereignty requirements, compliance obligations, and geographic constraints.

2. Design Architecture

Plan local deployment architecture with appropriate security controls and network isolation measures.

3. Deploy Securely

Implement Meetily with appropriate security controls, access management, and compliance documentation.

4. Monitor & Audit

Establish ongoing monitoring, compliance verification, and regular auditing of data sovereignty controls.

Our Story

How a team of consultants and engineers built a privacy-first meeting assistant when the tools we needed simply didn't exist.

Built by consultants, for people who value privacy
Our Vision

We believe your data should reside in your infrastructure. Meetily is built for professionals and organizations who share this vision. Those who want privacy-first AI without compromising on capabilities. We're democratizing access to powerful AI tools while respecting data sovereignty.
What We Stand For

The principles that guide everything we build

Privacy First, Always

Your data belongs to you. We don't use your conversations to train our models. Your data isn't our business model. Your privacy is. We're building AI tools that serve you without exploiting your information.

Open Source Philosophy

We believe in transparency and community. Our open-source approach means you can verify, modify, and trust what Meetily does with your data.

Democratizing AI

Privacy-first AI shouldn't be a luxury. We're making enterprise-grade, self-hosted AI accessible to everyone who values data sovereignty.
How Meetily Came to Be

From internal tool to privacy-first solution

The Challenge: The Manual Process We Couldn't Abandon

As a team of consultants and engineers working with multiple companies, we handled sensitive information daily. We had countless meetings, and for a team that values communication above all, documentation was critical. We wrote meeting notes by hand after each session and sent them via email to ensure alignment. It was time-consuming, but we couldn't compromise on this practice.

The Attempt: Exploring Automated Solutions

We tried meeting note-takers to automate this workflow. The efficiency was appealing, until we examined where our data was going. All meeting data was being stored and processed in their private cloud infrastructure. For a team trusted with client confidential information, this was a non-starter. A convenience on our end meant exposing 3-4 meetings worth of sensitive data to external clouds.

The Discovery: Reading the Fine Print

We went through the privacy policies of every major meeting note-taker on the market. What we found was consistent: almost all of them use customer data to train their AI models, building what they call their 'AI model moat.' This wasn't just a privacy concern; it was fundamentally at odds with how we believe AI tools should be built. We decided to wait for a solution that aligned with our values.

Privacy-first from day one
The Solution: Building What We Needed

When the right tool didn't emerge, we built it ourselves. A straightforward meeting note-taker that runs entirely on our computers, no cloud storage, no third-party data access. We started using it internally, and it worked well. Our clients began noticing something: we were capturing details that others missed, even in complex technical discussions.

The Validation: From Internal Tool to Market Need

When clients asked how we managed such comprehensive note-taking, we shared our approach. One immediately requested access to the tool. Then another. We realized this wasn't just our problem, there were others who needed a privacy-first solution. We started building in public, and the response confirmed our hypothesis: the enquiries kept coming.

Thousands of users globally
Today: Meetily: Privacy-First AI at Scale

We spent months refining our internal tool into a robust product. Today, Meetily is used by thousands of professionals, and adoption continues to grow. We're committed to open source and self-hosted solutions. As our CEO says, we're focused on 'giving without expecting.' Our community edition is completely free, updated every two months with the latest features. Our pro version offers advanced capabilities for teams with specific needs.
Why Choose Meetily Pro?
Privacy-First Architecture

Transcription runs 100% locally; recordings and audio never leave your device. For summaries, you choose your provider: local AI or your own API key. Compliant by design with GDPR and HIPAA workflows.

Advanced AI Features

Real-time transcription, speaker identification, action item extraction, meeting summaries, and custom AI workflows.

Priority Support

Dedicated support team, direct access to engineers, custom integration assistance, and priority bug fixes.

Flexible Deployment

On-Device deployment. Local file access for custom integrations and workflows.

Frequently Asked Questions
Is Meetily really open source?
Yes, Meetily is fully open source under the MIT license. You can view, modify, and distribute the source code freely. Our GitHub repository is public and we welcome contributions from the community.
What does 'local processing' mean?
Local processing means all transcription, AI summarization, and data storage happens on your own device. Your meeting audio and transcripts never leave your computer or are sent to external cloud servers.
Is Meetily really free?
Yes, the Community edition is free for personal use. No subscription fees, usage limits, or hidden costs. Pro edition with premium features is available for those who want enhanced capabilities.
Can I self-host Meetily?
Meetily runs locally on your desktop by default. For organizations wanting centralized deployment, you can self-host on your own infrastructure with complete control over data and configuration.
How does Meetily compare to Otter.ai or Fireflies?
Unlike cloud services that send your audio to external servers, Meetily processes everything locally. This means complete privacy, works offline, Community Edition free, and GDPR/HIPAA compliant by design.
What platforms does Meetily support?
Meetily currently supports Windows and macOS as native desktop apps. It works with any meeting platform including Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, Webex, and more. A native Linux desktop app is in development - join the Linux waitlist at /granola-for-linux. For Linux desktop today, build the app bundle from source via the open-source repo.

Our Privacy-First Approach

Built on principles of local processing, complete transparency, and user control

100% Local Transcription

Audio capture and transcription run on your device by default, and on that path audio never leaves your machine. Pro users can choose pluggable summary providers (local AI or BYOK). Community Edition keeps everything local end-to-end.

Complete Transparency

Open source code you can inspect. No hidden data collection, community-driven development, and regular security audits.

Zero Audio Collection

Meeting audio and recordings stay on your device by default, on every tier. We never collect personal information from meeting content. With BYOK summaries (Pro, optional), only transcript text is sent to your chosen provider; audio is never sent.

Data Sovereignty

Data remains within your chosen jurisdiction. No foreign government access risks, complete organizational control, and automatic compliance with local laws.

Full Data Control

Access all your data locally, export in standard formats, move data between systems, and no vendor lock-in.

User Rights

Delete data at any time, set retention policies, control sharing, and modify processing settings as you need.
Data Collection & Usage

Complete transparency about what we do and don't collect

What We DON'T Collect
×
Meeting audio or video content (always stays local)
×
Transcripts when using local or BYOK summaries
×
Stored summaries or notes (we don't retain them)
×
Personal information from meetings
×
Usage analytics or telemetry without consent

Pro users who choose a BYOK provider: transcript text is sent to your chosen provider only to generate the summary you requested. Audio never leaves your device. Retention is governed by your provider; see /llm-privacy.

What We DO Collect (Optional)
✓
Anonymous usage statistics (opt-in)
✓
Crash reports (anonymized)
✓
Feature usage patterns (no content)
✓
Performance metrics

All analytics are completely optional and can be disabled in settings.
Trial Journey & Website Data Flows

Transparent disclosure of the three new data flows introduced with the Trial Journey Hub

License status check (Trial Journey Hub)

When you visit your trial dashboard at /my-license, we may check whether your trial is active, expired, or pending. To do this, your license identifier (licenseId) - a UUID embedded in your RSA-signed license key payload - is sent from your browser to a Meetily server-side function, which queries the license backend.

+
What is sent: your licenseId (UUID only - no email, no name, no payment data).
+
What comes back: non-personal status only - license state (active/expired/pending) and expiry date. Your email, name, or any other personal information is never returned to the browser by this endpoint.
+
API key: the backend API key used to authenticate this lookup is held server-side only and is never sent to your browser.
+
Purpose: to route you to the correct state (e.g., "extend from the app" if activated-expired, or "get help" if lapsed before activation) without exposing personal data to the browser.
+
Retention: no log of the licenseId query is retained beyond the function invocation. The status result is used only to render the correct page state.
Help and support form (/help)

The trial help form at /help lets you contact our support team when you are stuck, have lost your key, or need help activating. When you submit the form:

+
What is collected: your email address, the help topic you select (e.g., "lost license key"), an optional free-text message, your operating system (auto-detected, editable), and app version (or "not installed").
+
Where it goes: your submission is delivered to the Meetily support team inbox (Zackriya Solutions) via Resend, a transactional email service. Your email address is set as the Reply-To on the team email, so our team can respond to you directly.
+
Purpose: to replace dead-end mailto: links with a triaged support channel that actually reaches a human and delivers a reply.
+
Retention: submissions are retained in our team email inbox for support follow-up. We do not store them in a separate database. You may request deletion by emailing meetily@zackriya.com.
+
Scope: this form is scoped to the Pro trial journey only. It is not a general contact form for open-source or community users.
Website analytics (meetily.ai)

The meetily.ai marketing website uses PostHog for product analytics. This is separate from the Meetily desktop app, which does not send analytics without your consent.

+
What is collected: page views, session information, button clicks, referrer URLs, and usage patterns on the website. No meeting audio, transcripts, or app content is collected here.
+
Server-side events: some events (such as trial license issuance) are sent from our server-side functions to PostHog using a hashed identifier, never your raw email address, in line with our no-raw-email-in-analytics policy.
+
Provider: PostHog (posthog.com). Data is processed in accordance with PostHog's privacy policy and data processing agreement.
+
Purpose: to understand how visitors navigate the marketing site and where the trial activation journey breaks down, so we can improve it.
Compliance & Standards

Built-in compliance with international data protection regulations

GDPR Compliant by Design

Data minimization by design, local transcription, right to erasure support, and privacy by default. We hold no formal certification - compliance is achieved through architecture.

HIPAA Compliant by Design

Local transcription, no audio sharing, administrative safeguards, and audit-friendly architecture. We hold no formal HIPAA certification.

SOC 2 Architecture

Security controls, availability measures, processing integrity, and confidentiality protection. Architecture supports SOC 2 - not formally certified.

Trial questions
How long does my Meetily Pro trial last?
The trial is 14 days of full Pro features, with no payment or card required. After it ends you have a 1-day grace period before Pro features lock.
Where do I find my license key?
Your key is saved on this device and emailed to you when you start the trial. Copy it from this page, download it as a .txt, or open your inbox for the message from Meetily.
How do I activate my trial?
Download Meetily for macOS or Windows, install it, and open the app. At the license step, paste your key to activate your 14-day Pro trial.
Do I have to activate right away?
You have 30 days from when the key is generated to activate it. After that the key expires, and you can request a fresh trial from this page.
Is my data private during the trial?
All your data processing, storage and recording happens locally. You own your data unless you choose external providers. See our privacy references for LLM and BYOK details.
What happens when the trial ends?
Take the time you need to be sure Meetily is the right fit. If your trial is running low, just reach out and we're glad to extend it. Whenever you're ready, subscribe to Pro to keep the Pro features, or keep using the free, open-source Community edition.

New in Meetily Pro 1.11.0
Meetily CLI: Meeting Notes From the Command Line

meetily-pro is the command-line tool that ships inside Meetily Pro 1.11.0. Your scripts can search meetings, export any meeting as Markdown, text or JSON, read transcripts and summaries, start or stop a recording, and subscribe to webhooks, all against Meetily's local API on your own computer. It stays off until you allow it, and it only gets the permissions you give it.

Start free trial
CLI docs
MEETILY-PRO
MARKDOWN, TEXT, JSON
LOCAL API
SCOPED KEYS
SHIPS WITH MEETILY PRO ON WINDOWS AND MACOS
READ-ONLY UNTIL YOU GIVE IT A KEY
OUTPUT YOUR SCRIPTS CAN PARSE
NO SDK NEEDED
The Meetily CLI, answered

Last updated: September 25, 2026 • Reviewed by the Meetily team

What is the Meetily CLI?

meetily-pro is Meetily’s own command-line tool, bundled with Meetily Pro 1.11.0 and later. It is the scripting side of the same integrations that power the Meetily MCP server: a local API on your computer, per-key permissions, and signed webhooks. Use it from a shell script, cron, a task runner or any language that can run a command, while Meetily is running. It does not transcribe anything itself; Meetily does that on your computer, and the CLI reads and controls the results.

How do I turn on the Meetily CLI?
Open Settings > PRO and turn on Integrations.
In the Integrations tab, under Apps & scripts, turn on Allow the CLI on this computer. That gives the CLI read-only access to your meetings and recording notifications.
If a script needs to start or stop recordings, click Create key, give it only the permissions it needs, copy the key (Meetily shows it once) and turn on its Allow switch.
Allowing the CLI gives it read-only access. Recording control needs a key.

The CLI docs show where the binary lives on Windows and macOS. meetily-pro install-cli copies it to ~/.local/bin and tells you if that folder still needs adding to your PATH.

Which commands does meetily-pro have?

The ones most scripts need, with the permission each one uses:

meetily-pro commands and the permission each needs
Task	Command	Permission
Search every transcript	meetily-pro search "pricing page"	Read
List recent meetings	meetily-pro meetings list --limit 10	Read
Export a meeting (md, txt or json)	meetily-pro meetings export <meeting-id> --format md	Read
Read a transcript	meetily-pro transcript get <meeting-id>	Read
Read a summary	meetily-pro summary get <meeting-id>	Read
Check whether Meetily is recording	meetily-pro recording status	Read
Wait (up to an hour) until a recording stops	meetily-pro recording wait --until stopped --wait-timeout 3600	Read
Start a recording	meetily-pro recording start --meeting-name "Weekly sync"	Record
Stop the recording	meetily-pro recording stop	Record
Get a webhook when a summary is ready	meetily-pro webhooks add --url <url> --events summary.completed	Read
Check your setup	meetily-pro doctor	None

Every command accepts --json for raw JSON output. The full list, including jobs, speaker labels and summary regeneration, is in the CLI reference.

How do I export a meeting to Markdown?

meetings export prints the meeting’s title, summary and timestamped transcript. Pick Markdown, plain text or JSON (the default), and redirect it wherever you keep notes, for example an Obsidian vault:

Code language:
bash
meetily-pro meetings list --limit 5
meetily-pro meetings export <meeting-id> --format md > ~/Obsidian/Meetings/weekly-sync.md

See it in use: a consultant’s second brain in Obsidian, built on Meetily transcripts.

How can a script react when a meeting ends?

Two ways. For a quick script, block until the recording stops and carry on from there. For something that runs all the time, subscribe a local endpoint to a signed webhook:

Code language:
bash
# Block (up to an hour) until the current recording stops
meetily-pro recording wait --until stopped --wait-timeout 3600

# Or get a signed webhook every time a summary is ready
meetily-pro webhooks add --url http://127.0.0.1:9002/meetily --events summary.completed

Before you add a webhook, turn on Outgoing (webhooks) and add your script’s address as a local target under Integrations > Advanced. A new webhook then stays pending until you approve its destination, and Meetily shows its signing secret once, when you add it. Each delivery carries only the event name, a meeting ID and a timestamp, signed with HMAC-SHA256 in the X-Meetily-Signature header. Details are in the webhooks docs.

How does the CLI authenticate?

Allowing the CLI in the app is enough for read-only commands on the same computer. For anything more, create a key in Apps & scripts with only the permissions the script needs, copy it (Meetily shows it once), turn on its Allow switch, save it to a file, and pass it with --token-file or the MEETILY_PRO_TOKEN environment variable. The CLI no longer accepts a key directly on the command line, so it never shows up in your shell history or process list.

Code language:
bash
meetily-pro --token-file ~/.config/meetily/record.key recording start --meeting-name "Design review"
Want ready-made scripts? The meetily-workflows repository has setup guides with screenshots and example workflows, and Meetily workflows walks through recipes for Obsidian, Notion, Jira, Slack and your CRM.

Meetily CLI: Common Questions
Is the Meetily CLI free?
The meetily-pro command-line tool is part of Meetily Pro ($10/user/month billed annually) and is included in the 14-day Pro trial. The free Community Edition does not include it.
Does the Meetily CLI work on Windows and macOS?
Yes. The meetily-pro binary ships inside Meetily Pro on both Windows and macOS (Apple Silicon). The CLI docs show where it lives and how to put it on your PATH.
Does the CLI send my meetings anywhere?
No. The CLI talks to Meetily on your own computer; the API listens only there by default. Meeting notes leave the computer only if your script sends them. Webhooks carry only the event name, a meeting ID and a timestamp.
Is there a Python or JavaScript SDK?
There is no official SDK. Use the meetily-pro command-line tool from any script, or call the local HTTP API directly from any language. The meetily-workflows repository includes small example helpers.
Can a script delete meetings with the CLI?
Only with a key that has the Delete permission. Even then, meetily-pro meetings delete asks for --yes when it runs from a script, and it keeps the recording files unless you also pass --delete-files.
Does Meetily need to be running for the CLI to work?
Yes. The CLI talks to the running app's local API, so Meetily has to be open with Integrations turned on before a script or scheduled job runs.
How do I get output my script can parse?
Add --json to any command for raw JSON instead of human-readable output. meetings export writes Markdown or plain text with --format md or --format txt, and JSON by default.

Frequently Asked Questions
What is local-first meeting transcription?
Local-first meeting transcription processes audio entirely on your device using AI models like Whisper. No audio files are uploaded to any server. The transcription engine runs on your CPU or GPU, and the output stays on your local disk. This approach eliminates third-party data access and jurisdictional complications.
Is local AI transcription as accurate as cloud-based services?
Yes. Open-source models like OpenAI's Whisper have reached parity with cloud transcription services. Whisper supports 99+ languages with high accuracy. For summarization, 4B parameter models can handle structured tasks like action item extraction effectively. Meetily also lets you bring your own API key (BYOK) for cloud summarization if you want additional quality.
How does local transcription help with GDPR and HIPAA compliance?
When transcription runs locally, personal data and protected health information never leave your device. This eliminates the need for data processing agreements with transcription vendors, removes cross-border data transfer concerns, and gives you full control over data retention and deletion. Meetily is GDPR and HIPAA compliant by design.
Can I still get AI meeting summaries with local-first tools?
Yes. Meetily offers two options for AI summaries: fully local models via Ollama or Built-in AI, or bring-your-own-key (BYOK) with Claude, OpenAI, or Groq. Transcription is always 100% local regardless of which summary option you choose.
Does local meeting transcription work offline?
Yes. After the initial model download, Meetily's transcription works completely offline. You can record and transcribe meetings on a plane, in a secure facility, or anywhere without internet. If you use local AI models for summarization, those work offline too.
What hardware do I need for local meeting transcription?
Minimum: 8GB RAM and a modern 4-core CPU. Recommended: 16GB RAM with GPU acceleration for faster processing. Meetily runs on Windows and macOS (M Series). Most laptops from the last 5 years handle real-time transcription without issues. You can choose smaller or larger AI models based on your hardware.

Frequently Asked Questions
What is Meetily?
Meetily is an open-source AI meeting assistant built by Zackriya Solutions. It records and transcribes meetings on your own device instead of uploading the audio to a vendor's servers, and it does it without anything joining the call - it captures your system audio, so it works with Zoom, Teams, Meet, Discord, or any other app that produces sound. The code is on GitHub under the MIT license, and the Community Edition is free.
Who built Meetily, and why?
Zackriya Solutions, a software company, first built it for internal use. A client said they were not comfortable with a bot in the meeting, and every tool available at the time uploaded meeting audio to its own servers. We built something that kept the audio on our own machines, used it in client meetings, and then open-sourced it. The first commit went to GitHub on December 26, 2024.
Does Meetily use a meeting bot?
No. Meetily captures audio from your own device, so nothing joins the call as a participant. There is no extra attendee in the list and nothing to admit from the waiting room.
Where does my meeting audio go?
By default it stays on your machine. Transcription runs on your device and recordings are stored there. Meetily Pro also offers an opt-in cloud transcription option; if you switch that on, the audio goes to the provider you chose, under your own account. For summaries you pick the model - a local one, or your own API key, in which case only the transcript text goes to that provider under their terms. None of it routes through Zackriya Solutions.
Is Meetily free?
The Community Edition is free and open source under the MIT license. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually and adds features, not a different transcription engine - every edition runs the same one. Enterprise pricing is custom.
What platforms does Meetily run on today?
Windows and macOS have native desktop apps. The very first version in early 2025 was macOS only; Windows arrived shortly after. There is no native Linux build today.

Why your meeting data belongs on your device
Read the post
Frequently Asked Questions
Is there a FERPA-compliant AI note taker?
There's no FERPA certification, so no AI note taker can honestly call itself FERPA certified. FERPA sets duties for schools, and a vendor that receives student records has to work as a school official under the school's direct control. The safest setup is a tool that never receives student data at all. Meetily transcribes on the staff member's own computer by default and only receives license information, and we've signed a California student data privacy agreement. Your district's counsel makes the final call for your situation.
Is a SOC 2 or HIPAA-compliant AI note taker automatically FERPA compliant?
No. SOC 2 and HIPAA are about how a vendor protects the data it holds. FERPA asks a different question: should the vendor hold student records at all, and under what contract. A vendor with strong security still has to act as a school official under the school's direct control, with limits on how it uses and shares the records. The simplest way to meet that duty is a tool that never receives the records. Meetily transcribes on the staff member's computer by default and only receives license information.
Can I record an IEP meeting with an AI note taker?
Federal law doesn't say yes or no. The US Department of Education says no federal law authorizes or prohibits recording an IEP meeting, and states and districts may regulate it (64 FR 12477, Appendix A, question 21
(opens in new tab)
). In California, the parent and the district both have the right to audio-record an IEP meeting with at least 24 hours' notice to the team, and if the district wants to record and the parent objects, the meeting isn't recorded (Education Code 56341.1(g)
(opens in new tab)
). If the school keeps the recording, it's an education record under FERPA and IDEA. Check your district's policy first, tell everyone in the meeting, and treat any AI summary as a draft a person reviews.
Is an AI meeting transcript an education record under FERPA?
It can be. Under FERPA, an education record is a record directly related to a student and kept by the school or by someone acting for it (34 CFR 99.3
(opens in new tab)
). A transcript or summary of a meeting about a student that's saved in a school system, or on a vendor's servers working for the school, usually fits. There's a narrow exception for notes kept only by the person who wrote them as a memory aid and never shown to anyone else, but notes that are shared or stored in a school system lose it.
Does HIPAA apply to IEP and special education meetings?
Generally not in K-12 schools. The HIPAA Privacy Rule excludes records covered by FERPA, and IEP records are education records under FERPA and IDEA (HHS and Department of Education joint guidance, 2019
(opens in new tab)
). So the document that matters with a vendor is usually a student data privacy agreement, not a HIPAA business associate agreement (BAA).
Do I need consent to record a parent-teacher conference?
Often, yes. California, for example, makes it an offense to record a confidential conversation without the consent of everyone in it (Penal Code 632
(opens in new tab)
), and several other states have similar all-party rules. Your district may also have its own policy. Since Meetily doesn't put a bot in the call, other people won't see a recording notice, so say you're recording at the start and make sure everyone agrees. Our guide to compliant meeting notes without a bot covers how to tell people.
What should I ask an AI note-taker vendor before using it with student data?
Ask where the audio, transcripts and summaries go, and who stores them. Ask whether the tool joins meetings on its own or reads calendars, and whether it sends notes to participants automatically. Ask whether district data is ever used to train AI models, for advertising or for resale, and get the answer in writing. Ask for the list of subprocessors that receive meeting content, how retention and deletion work, and whether the vendor will sign your state's student data privacy agreement. Then look at the agreement's schedule of data: the shorter it is, the less there is to protect.
Do I need a data privacy agreement to use an AI note taker with student data?
If the vendor will receive student data, your district will almost always want one, and many states require certain terms in the contract. California's Education Code section 49073.1 is one example. Most districts use their state's version of the National Data Privacy Agreement. Ask your district's privacy or IT team before you start: many keep a list of approved tools that already have a signed agreement. Meetily has signed the California version, and its schedule of data is almost empty because we only receive license information.
What does Meetily receive when a school buys licenses?
Only what's needed to manage the license: the license holder's name, email address and organization, the license key, the number of seats, a device ID used to enforce seat limits, and the app version. Meetily doesn't receive audio, transcripts, summaries, voiceprints or student data. By default, transcription and summaries run on the staff member's computer. If a school chooses an outside AI model for summaries, the transcript text goes to that provider under the school's own account, not to Meetily.
Does Meetily use school data to train AI models?
No. Meetily never receives meeting content, so there's nothing for us to train on. We've also put it in writing for schools: district data is never used to train AI models, for advertising or for resale.
Can other California districts sign Meetily's student data privacy agreement?
Yes. Our California agreement includes a General Offer of Privacy Terms, so another California district can sign on to the same privacy terms through the Student Data Privacy Consortium registry instead of negotiating a new agreement. It covers the privacy terms only; pricing and the service agreement are separate.
Does California's AB 1159 change anything for AI note takers?
AB 1159, signed in September 2026, expands California's student privacy laws. It bars covered ed tech companies from using student information to train generative AI or develop AI models, and it adds a new law for colleges and universities. The K-12 changes take effect on January 1, 2027, and the higher education rules on July 1, 2027 (Venable summary
(opens in new tab)
, CalMatters
(opens in new tab)
). For a tool that sends meetings to a vendor, that's a rule the vendor has to follow. Meetily never receives meeting content, so there's no student data on our side to train on, and our California agreement already says district data is never used to train AI models.
Does COPPA apply to an AI note taker teachers use?
COPPA covers online services that collect personal information from children under 13. Meetily is installed and used by staff on their own computers, and it doesn't collect personal information from students. The FTC's 2025 update to the COPPA Rule
(opens in new tab)
 didn't add a new school-consent rule; it kept its existing guidance for schools and ed tech.
Can I use AI transcription for IRB-approved research interviews?
Often, if your IRB protocol allows it. IRBs approve research only when there are adequate provisions to protect participants' privacy and keep the data confidential (45 CFR 46.111
(opens in new tab)
). A tool that uploads interview audio to a vendor adds a third party your data plan has to cover. With Meetily, transcription runs on the researcher's computer by default, so recordings stay under your study's own storage rules. Two things to check in your approved protocol before you start: some IRBs want research data only on a university-managed computer, not a personal laptop, and some want the consent form to say how recordings are transcribed.
How do I stop AI note-taker bots from joining district meetings?
Most districts start in the admin settings of their meeting platform, by limiting which third-party apps staff can connect and who can join meetings without being let in. Then they write down which AI tools are approved for which meetings, and give staff an approved option, so people aren't tempted to sign up for a bot on their own. A tool that runs on the staff member's computer, with no bot and no calendar access, never shows up in the meeting to begin with.
Which computers does Meetily run on?
Meetily runs on Windows and Mac. Linux users can build it from the open-source code today, and a Linux installer is in development. Meetily Enterprise can run on the institution's own servers, and IT can install the app silently on staff computers.
