/blog/eu-ai-act-meeting-tools-risk-level
H1: Your Meeting Tool's Risk Level Under the EU AI Act Isn't Set by the Vendor. It's Set by You.
H2: Frequently Asked Questions
H3: Ready to try Meetily?
H2: Get Started with Meetily
H3: Meetily Pro
H3: Download
H3: Meetily Pro
H3: Download
H3: Open Source
H2: Recent Articles
H3: AI Meeting Notes for Schools: Why the Safest Student Data Agreement Has Almost Nothing to Cover
H3: How to Record and Transcribe a Teams Meeting Without a Bot
H3: Why We Built Meetily When Every Meeting Tool Already Existed
New
Why your meeting data belongs on your device
Read the post
Frequently Asked Questions
What are the risk levels of the EU AI Act?
There are four risk levels, also called risk categories. Unacceptable risk is banned outright, for example social scoring or reading people's emotions at work. High risk is allowed with strict duties, for example AI used in hiring or to evaluate workers. Limited risk carries light transparency duties, for example telling people they're talking to a chatbot or labeling AI-generated content. Minimal risk, like a spam filter, has no new duties. An AI meeting tool that transcribes and summarizes is usually minimal or limited risk. The same tool used to score staff is high risk.
Are AI meeting note takers high-risk under the EU AI Act?
Not by itself. The AI Act looks at what a tool is used for, not at the product. Recording a call, writing a transcript and producing a summary isn't on the Act's high-risk list. It becomes high-risk when the output is used to monitor or evaluate workers, or to make decisions about their promotion, dismissal or tasks. Those uses are listed in Annex III, point 4.
Can my employer use meeting transcripts to evaluate my performance?
Under the AI Act, using AI to monitor or evaluate workers' performance and behavior is a high-risk use. From 2 December 2027, an employer doing this must follow the vendor's instructions, keep a human in charge of the decisions, and tell workers' representatives and the affected workers before using it (Article 26). The GDPR and national employment law already apply today, even to a small pilot. In Germany, for example, the works council must agree before an employer introduces technical tools that can monitor staff behavior or performance (Section 87(1) no. 6 of the Works Constitution Act).
What is required for high-risk AI systems under the EU AI Act?
The vendor, called the provider, has to manage risks, use good-quality data, keep technical documentation and logs, design the system for human oversight, meet accuracy and security standards, run a quality management system (the part EN 18286 covers), pass a conformity assessment and register the system. The company using it, called the deployer, has to use it as instructed, give oversight to trained people, keep the logs it produces for at least six months, inform workers before using it at work, and run a data protection impact assessment where the GDPR requires one. For workplace uses, these duties start on 2 December 2027.
Do we have to tell people an AI note taker is in the meeting?
Yes. The GDPR says people must be told when their personal data is collected and why, and a meeting recording or transcript is personal data. If the output is used for a high-risk purpose at work, such as evaluating staff, the AI Act adds its own duty from December 2027 to inform workers and their representatives first. Some countries go further. In Germany, for example, secretly recording someone's spoken words can be a criminal offense under Section 201 of the Criminal Code
(opens in new tab)
. We cover how to tell people in compliant meeting notes without a bot.
Is emotion recognition in work meetings banned under the EU AI Act?
Yes. Since 2 February 2025, Article 5 of the AI Act
(opens in new tab)
 bans AI that infers the emotions of people at work or in education from biometric data, such as their tone of voice or their face on camera. The only exceptions are medical or safety reasons. It's one of a short list of banned uses, alongside social scoring and manipulative AI. According to the Commission's guidelines, inferring emotions from written text alone, such as the words in a transcript, isn't covered by the ban (Future of Privacy Forum summary
(opens in new tab)
).
Has the EU AI Act been delayed to 2027?
Partly, and the delay is now law. The Digital Omnibus, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744, in force since 27 July 2026, moved the main rules for high-risk AI listed in Annex III, which includes workplace uses, to 2 December 2027. High-risk AI built into regulated products moves to 2 August 2028. The bans in Article 5 and the AI literacy duty weren't delayed. Both have applied since 2 February 2025.
What is EN 18286?
EN 18286 is the first harmonized standard under the EU AI Act. It sets out how providers of high-risk AI should run a quality management system, the requirement in Article 17. It was published in the Official Journal of the EU on 14 October 2026, through Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2026/2274. A provider that follows it is presumed to meet that part of the law. Using it's voluntary, and standards for other areas, such as risk management and cybersecurity, are still being written.
Who is responsible under the AI Act: the meeting tool vendor or the company using it?
Both, for different things. The vendor, which the Act calls the provider, answers for how the tool is built. The company using it, which the Act calls the deployer, answers for how it's used. Buying from a careful vendor doesn't cover you if you use the tool's output for a high-risk purpose, such as rating employees.
Who needs to comply with the EU AI Act?
Anyone who builds, sells or uses AI in a professional setting in the EU. That covers providers, which build the system or put it on the market, and deployers, which use it at work, plus importers and distributors. It also reaches companies outside the EU when their AI is sold in the EU or its output is used there (Article 2). So a US company with staff or customers in Europe should assume it applies. Purely personal, non-professional use is out of scope.
What does the AI literacy duty mean for teams using AI meeting tools?
Article 4 asks companies that use AI to take steps so their people understand the tools they use, including what the tools can get wrong and what they must not be used for. Since the Digital Omnibus, the duty is about making the effort, not reaching a fixed level for each person. A short internal record of the training you have done is a sensible way to show it.
Does running a meeting tool on our own devices make us compliant?
No. Where the data sits doesn't decide how the tool is used, so local processing can't make anyone compliant on its own. What it does is remove some questions. With Meetily, transcription runs on your device by default, so there is no audio transfer to another country and no vendor copy of the recording. For summaries, you pick a local model or your own API key with a provider you choose. If you use your own key, the transcript text goes to that provider under your account.
Does the GDPR still apply to AI meeting recordings?
Yes. Nothing in the AI Act replaces the GDPR. A recording of people who can be identified is personal data. So the usual questions still apply: your legal basis, such as consent, the purpose, how long you keep it, and where it's stored. Our GDPR guide for meeting transcription walks through each one.

Frequently asked questions
Can I record or transcribe a Teams meeting if I'm not the host or organizer?
Yes, if you're in the organizer's company, your account has permission, and the meeting options let you. Guests and people from other companies can't. If the options are locked, ask the organizer to record or share the transcript. Or record on your own computer, if everyone agrees and your company allows it.
Can I record or transcribe a Teams meeting just for myself?
A Teams recording is saved to the organizer's OneDrive (or the channel's SharePoint site), and people in the organizer's company can view it. If you want a private record that isn't a shared Teams transcript, a desktop recorder saves the file on your own laptop. You still need to tell the others in the call.
Is there a way to transcribe a Teams meeting without everyone knowing?
An app on your own computer doesn't show anything in Teams. That doesn't make it OK to record people without telling them. In many places it's against the law, and it breaks most company policies. Tell everyone and get their agreement first. Meetily reminds you to do this every time you start a recording.
Will Teams or my IT team know if I use a recording app?
Teams doesn't post anything in the meeting when a separate app records your computer's audio. But your company's device-management tools may see which apps are installed or running. Don't count on going unnoticed. Follow your company's policy.
Is it legal to record a Teams meeting?
It depends on where you and the other people are. Some places need everyone's consent, and others need only one person's. The safe habit is to tell everyone and record only if they agree. This isn't legal advice, so check the laws that apply to you and your company's policy.
Can I record or transcribe a Teams meeting for free?
Yes. If your work or school plan lets you record, recording and transcription are included, and you don't need Copilot or Teams Premium. On Teams Free, recording needs a paid Microsoft 365 plan, and Microsoft documents live captions but not saved transcripts. Meetily Community is a free, open-source app that records and transcribes Teams calls on your own computer.
What's the difference between recording and transcription in Teams?
A recording is a video or audio file. A transcript is the text of what people said, with their names and timestamps. You can have both, or run a transcript on its own.
Can Teams record and transcribe automatically?
Yes, if your admin allows it. The organizer turns on Record and transcribe automatically in the meeting options for each meeting. It's off by default. See Microsoft's page on automatic recording
(opens in new tab)
.
Does my audio go to the cloud when I record on my own computer?
Not with Meetily's default setup. The audio is recorded and transcribed on your computer. For summaries, you choose a local model or your own API key. If you use your own key, the transcript text goes to that AI provider to be summarized.
Does Meetily work with Teams in a web browser?
Yes. Meetily records your computer's system audio, not just one app. So it captures a Teams call in Chrome or Edge the same way it captures the desktop app.
Can I use Meetily for an in-person meeting?
Yes. Start a recording, and Meetily uses your microphone to capture the room. For more tips, see our guide to recording a meeting.
How do I transcribe a Teams recording I already have?
If you own the recording or can edit it, open it and use Video settings → Generate to make a transcript in Microsoft's player. See Microsoft's page on video transcripts
(opens in new tab)
. Or, if you're allowed to download it, save the recording from OneDrive and open Import Audio in Meetily. It takes common audio and video files, including MP4, WAV and MP3, and transcribes them on your computer. Here's how importing works.

A client told us they weren't comfortable with a bot in the meeting. We looked for a tool that kept the audio on our own machines, found none, and built one.

Frequently Asked Questions
What is a second brain for meeting notes?

A second brain is a personal system for keeping what you learn outside your head, so you can find it when you need it.

For meeting notes, it means keeping conversations, decisions, and action items in one place, linked to the right client or project. You can look back at what was discussed, what was agreed, and who needs to do what.

How do I build a second brain like this with Meetily?

You can connect Meetily to Obsidian and an action-item tracker with a small script. Here’s how it works:

Meetily records the meeting and transcribes it on your own Windows or Mac computer. No bot joins the call.
When the summary is ready, Meetily sends your script a signed webhook, an automatic notice that carries the meeting ID but not the notes.
The script gets the meeting title, summary, and timestamped transcript from the local Meetily API. For a quick one-off, the meetily-pro CLI does the same in one command: meetily-pro meetings export <meeting-id> --format md.
An AI model of your choice, either a local model or a provider you pick with your own API key, turns the export into a structured note and a list of action items.
The script saves the note in your Obsidian vault and adds the action items to your tracker, each with an owner and a deadline.

Meetily must be running for this to work. The Meetily API is off by default, so turn it on and set up your own script.

The Obsidian recipe walks you through the setup. The Meetily API docs
(opens in new tab)
 have the full reference, the Meetily CLI guide covers quick commands, and the meetily-workflows GitHub repository
(opens in new tab)
 has example scripts.

Does Meetily work with Obsidian?

Yes. Meetily exports meeting notes as Markdown files that you save straight into your Obsidian vault, with no plugin needed.

A webhook tells your script when a summary is ready, and the script gets the note from the local Meetily API
(opens in new tab)
 (or the meetily-pro CLI), so each note lands in your vault without copying and pasting. Meetily must be running, and your own script handles saving the file.

Follow the Obsidian recipe for the setup instructions.

Can I track action items from meeting transcripts automatically?

Yes. Meetily’s summaries include action items, and your script can fetch them from the local Meetily API
(opens in new tab)
 as JSON, a structured format that’s easy for scripts to work with.

The script can then add those items to your own database or task tracker, with an owner and a deadline for each one. You can then use a dashboard to see what’s overdue, what’s due this week, and what’s slipping.

Is it safe to use AI note takers for confidential meetings?

It depends on where the recording, transcript, and summary go. Check that against your company's confidentiality policy and any client agreements, such as NDAs. Meetily records and transcribes on your own computer by default, and no bot joins the call.

Summaries made with a local model stay on your machine. If you use an AI provider with your own API key instead, the transcript text goes to that provider. See how each AI provider handles your data.

The Meetily API is off until you turn it on. By default it only listens on your own computer, and webhooks carry a meeting ID, not the notes.

Can Meetily summarize a meeting without an internet connection?

Yes. Once you’ve downloaded a local AI model, Meetily can generate meeting summaries on your own computer without an internet connection.

If you use an AI provider with your own API key instead, only the transcript text is sent to that provider.

See how to choose a summary model for more details.

Can I ask Claude or Cursor about my past meetings?

Yes. The Meetily MCP server, built on the same Meetily API, lets AI assistants connect to your meeting data.

You can connect Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or Windsurf, choose what each one is allowed to do, and ask questions like “What did we decide last week?”

Most assistants send what they read to their own AI provider, so only connect the ones you already trust with your meeting notes.

Frequently Asked Questions
Does OpenAI train on my data?
Not on anything you send through the API. That's been the default since March 2023. It does train on consumer ChatGPT conversations on Free, Plus and Pro personal accounts, unless you switch off "Improve the model for everyone" under Settings, then Data Controls. Switching it off applies to new conversations, and you keep your chat history either way. Business, Enterprise and Edu accounts aren't trained on by default. Which privacy policy covers you also depends on your region, because OpenAI publishes separate Europe, US and rest-of-world versions with different legal entities named.
Is the API different from the consumer app?
Often, and usually in the same direction. The API is stricter. OpenAI, Google, Mistral, Perplexity and xAI all train on at least one free or consumer tier while saying they don't train on paid API traffic. Approving a provider on its API terms doesn't approve the app your team is actually opening.
If they don't train on my data, do they still keep it?
Usually, for a period. OpenAI holds API traffic for 30 days for abuse monitoring, unless you have zero data retention. Anthropic's consumer default is 30 days, and up to five years if you turn on model improvement. The Gemini app deletes activity at 18 months, but keeps human-reviewed chats for up to three years. Together AI stores prompts and responses by default while not training on them. DeepSeek publishes no deletion timeline at all.
Do AI meeting note-takers train on my recordings?
It's split. Fireflies and tl;dv both say customer content isn't used to train AI models. Granola trains only on de-identified data and lets you opt out. Otter's policy says it does use de-identified recordings and transcriptions to train its own AI. For all of them, the bigger question is collection rather than training, because a cloud note-taker has to receive and store your recording, and usually your participant list and calendar with it. Most also pass your meeting to outside transcription and AI companies, under contracts between them and those companies.
What does Meetily send to my chosen provider?
Transcript text, and only if you've set up bring-your-own-key summaries. Audio stays on your device unless you've deliberately switched on Pro's optional cloud transcription. If you use the built-in on-device model or Ollama, nothing is sent at all.
Does Meetily train on my meetings?
No. Your recordings and transcripts stay on your machine and we never receive them, so there's nothing for us to train on.
Which AI provider should I use?
We're not going to rank them. We sell one of the options on this page, so a ranking from us would be worth less than the terms themselves. The tables are here so you can check the tier you're actually on against the promise you think you have.

Frequently Asked Questions
Does Meetily work out who is speaking from their voice, or from their account?

From the audio. Meetily listens to the recording and separates it into different speakers based on their voices. It labels them Speaker 1, Speaker 2, and so on. You can then name each speaker yourself, and those names carry over to the transcript and summary and are remembered for future meetings.

There is no voiceprint database, and Meetily doesn't match speakers against calendar invitations or account information. That's also why it works the same way with an imported recording as it does with a live call.

Is speaker diarization included in the free Community Edition?

No. Speaker diarization and speaker identification are Meetily Pro features, introduced in Pro 1.8.2 on 22 July 2026. They are also included in Enterprise.

The free, MIT-licensed Community Edition gives you local transcription and AI summaries, but it doesn't separate the recording into different speakers or label who said what. You can try Pro with a 14-day trial, no card required.

Does speaker diarization run on my own machine?

Yes. Diarization runs on-device, as part of the same local pipeline that handles transcription with Whisper or Parakeet. Your audio doesn't need to leave your machine to figure out who spoke when.

Summaries are a separate choice. You can run a local model, in which case nothing leaves your machine, or use your own API key, in which case only the transcript text is sent to the provider you choose.

Does Meetily prompt me when a meeting starts and stops?

Yes. Meetily Pro can detect when a meeting starts and ends and prompt you, so you don't get twenty minutes into a call before realizing you forgot to record.

Pro can also start and stop recording automatically, with a countdown that you can cancel. This is turned off by default and can be enabled in Settings. The Community Edition uses manual start and stop, either from the interface or with a keyboard shortcut.

Frequently Asked Questions
Does my AI meeting assistant vendor use my data to train their models?
Check the data processing agreement, not the marketing page. Search it for "train", "improve", "aggregate" and "de-identified", and read the sentences around every hit. A marketing page can be edited without notice; a contract clause can't. Check the vendor's model provider separately too, since most meeting tools call somebody else's model.
How do I find out which AI meeting tools are already being used in my company?
Open your meeting platform's admin console and look at the participant logs for the last month. Notetaker bots join calls under their own names, so they appear as attendees who clearly aren't people. Google Meet, Zoom and Teams all retain this. That method only finds tools that join the call: a tool recording locally from an employee's laptop leaves no trace in your meeting platform, so pair it with a review of what's installed on managed devices.
What about Meetily's free tier, does it take my data?
No. Meetily Community runs transcription on your device, and it can run summaries on-device too. If you choose a cloud model with your own API key instead, the transcript text goes to that provider under their terms. Either way, no meeting content reaches Zackriya Solutions, so there is nothing for us to sell, share or train on. You can confirm the transcription half by disconnecting your network and watching it keep working.
Is Meetily's free tier really free?
Yes. Community Edition is free and open source under the MIT license, with no usage cap and no card required. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually, and Enterprise is custom priced. Community can be free because transcription runs on your hardware, so our costs don't go up as you use it more.
How does Meetily Community make money if not from my data?
It doesn't. Community is the open-source project. Zackriya Solutions earns revenue from Meetily Pro ($10/user/month billed annually) and from Enterprise deployments with self-hosting, admin controls and support. The free tier is funded by the paid tiers.
How is Meetily different from Granola, Fireflies and Otter?
The difference is where the processing happens. Meetily transcribes on your device and doesn't join your meeting as a bot. Those tools process in the cloud, and Fireflies and Otter join calls as participants. Meetily is also MIT licensed, so you can read the privacy claims in the code. Those tools have real advantages of their own, including bigger integration catalogs and longer-established support organizations.

Frequently Asked Questions
What's the difference between a privacy policy and a privacy architecture?
A policy is a promise about what a company will do with data it holds. An architecture decides whether the company ever holds the data at all. A policy can be broken by a breach, an acquisition, a compliance shortcut, or a court order, and none of those are things the company necessarily controls. An architecture that never collects the data in the first place can't be broken the same way, because there is nothing sitting on a server to expose.
Does Meetily ever see my meeting audio?
By default, recording and transcription both run on your own device, and the audio never leaves your machine. Meetily Pro also lets you point it somewhere else: an AI server you or your company run on your own infrastructure, or a provider you already use, with your own API key. In those cases the audio goes where you sent it, on machines you chose and under your own account. It never reaches us either way, so we have no copy of your recordings to breach, sell, or hand over in response to a legal order.
Where do the AI summaries run?
You choose. Meetily Pro connects to a local model running on your own hardware, a private LLM deployed on your company's own infrastructure, or a model provider you already use through your own API key. If you pick the last option, the transcript text goes to that provider under your key and your account. Whichever you choose, none of it routes through our servers.
Can I run the AI on my company's own infrastructure instead?
Yes. We still build that setup for teams who want it, with the models running on hardware you control and Meetily connecting to it. It just isn't a requirement any more. The reason Meetily works the way it does is that dedicated AI infrastructure priced out most of the people who needed the privacy most.

H3: How to Record and Transcribe a Teams Meeting Without a Bot

Why your meeting data belongs on your device
Read the post
Frequently Asked Questions
Is a meeting bot a third party under GDPR?
Yes. A meeting bot is operated by a vendor that processes personal data on your behalf, which makes that vendor a processor under the GDPR. You need a lawful basis, a data processing agreement, and a record of the processing, and you have to account for the vendor's own subprocessors. Recording locally on a device you already control doesn't introduce a processor, because no third party receives the data.
Does HIPAA require a business associate agreement for meeting bots?
If a meeting bot vendor receives protected health information, they are a business associate under HIPAA and a business associate agreement is required before they handle it. The practical difficulty is scope: a BAA governs how the vendor handles the data, not how many of their subcontractors touch it, and it doesn't reduce the number of places the recording now exists. If patient data never reaches a vendor, there's no business associate relationship to paper over in the first place.
What audit-log exposure does a meeting bot create?
When a bot joins a call, it appears as a participant in your meeting platform's audit log, alongside the humans. That record shows a third party attended, is retained under your platform's policy, and is discoverable in litigation or an audit. Local recording produces no such entry, because nothing joined the meeting.
If Meetily doesn't join as a bot, can I record without telling participants?
No. Your duty to tell people you're recording is set by law and by your own policies, and it doesn't change based on which tool you use. Meetily shows a notice on your own screen when recording starts, telling you to inform the other participants. You can dismiss it or switch it off in settings, and it behaves the same way on Community and Pro. It's a prompt to you, not an announcement to the meeting. Announcing is your responsibility, exactly as it is with any other recording method.
What are all-party consent states?
All-party consent states require everyone in a conversation to agree before it can be recorded, rather than just one participant. Federal law and most US states use one-party consent, where being a party to the conversation is enough. Around twelve states use all-party consent, including California, Illinois, Massachusetts and Washington, but the exact count varies by source because several states apply different rules to in-person conversation and to electronic communication. If participants are in different states, more than one rule can apply at once, so check your own jurisdictions with a lawyer. Announcing the recording at the start of the call satisfies the requirement everywhere.
Does GDPR require me to notify participants I'm using an AI notetaker?
Yes. Article 13 requires you to tell people whose personal data you collect who you are, why you're collecting it, and how long you'll keep it. A meeting recording is personal data, so participants have to be told before recording starts. Saying it out loud at the beginning of the call satisfies the substance of this. No tool can do it on your behalf.
Is Meetily a botless meeting recorder?
Yes. Meetily is a botless, or bot-free, meeting recorder: it captures audio from your own system rather than joining the call as a participant. Nothing appears in the attendee list and nothing connects to the meeting, so it works the same way across Zoom, Teams, Google Meet, Discord and any other platform.
Does Meetily support EU data residency?
European data residency is available on Meetily Enterprise, together with self-hosted deployment on your own infrastructure and managed compliance for GDPR, HIPAA and NIS2. On Community and Pro, transcription runs locally on your device by default, so audio and transcripts don't leave the machine regardless of region. Summaries depend on the provider you configure: a local model keeps everything on-device, while bringing your own API key sends transcript text to the provider you selected.
Is Meetily SOC 2 certified?
No. Meetily holds no SOC 2 certification today, and we don't claim one. What we offer instead is architectural: transcription runs locally on your device, Enterprise can be self-hosted entirely on your own infrastructure, and the source code is MIT licensed and open to audit at github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily. For organizations whose requirement is that meeting data never reaches a vendor, that's a stronger answer than a certification covering how a vendor handles data it has already received.

Frequently Asked Questions
What is the best way to record a meeting?
It depends on the job. For bot-free recording that works across platforms, use a local desktop recorder (Method 1). If everyone is on one platform you already pay for, use its built-in recorder (Method 2). For full visual control, use screen capture (Method 3). Tool-by-tool verdicts live in our full meeting recorder comparison.
What software can I use to record a meeting?
Four categories of software can capture a meeting: built-in platform recorders (Zoom, Google Meet, Microsoft Teams), desktop-based audio capture apps that record system sound with no bot in the call, cloud AI meeting assistants that join calls as bots, and screen recorders. Method 1 walks through the desktop-based audio capture path step by step.
Can ChatGPT record a meeting?
Partly. The ChatGPT desktop app for macOS offers a Record mode on paid plans: sessions cap at 240 minutes, processing happens in the cloud, and it is macOS only (OpenAI Help Center
(opens in new tab)
). For free local recording and transcription across Zoom, Meet, and Teams on Windows or macOS, use Method 1 with Meetily Community Edition.
How do I record a meeting without the host knowing?
Don't. Recording without consent can carry civil or criminal penalties in many jurisdictions. Bot-free tools exist to remove the awkward third-party bot from calls and keep recordings on your own device, not to enable secret recording. Announce the recording at the start, get agreement, then record.
Can I record a meeting I'm not hosting?
Yes, with local tools such as Meetily, OBS Studio, or Zoom local recording where the host permits it: recording happens on your own device. Built-in recorders on Google Meet and Microsoft Teams are typically restricted to hosts or organizers. Consent rules apply either way: tell the participants before you start.
How do I record a meeting on a free Google account?
Google Meet's built-in recorder is unavailable on free accounts. Instead, Meetily Community Edition records the Meet call's system audio for free, with local transcription, or OBS Studio captures the screen. Both run on your machine, so no Workspace upgrade is needed. Method 1 has the step-by-step setup.
Do I need internet to record and transcribe?
With Meetily, no: after the initial install and model download, recording and transcription work completely offline, which also suits air-gapped environments. Cloud recording tools such as tl;dv and Otter.ai need an internet connection to record and process, and Meet and Teams recordings save to their vendor's cloud.
Can a local recorder tell who said what?
Yes. Meetily Pro includes speaker identification, which labels each part of the transcript by speaker and runs on-device, so the audio is never uploaded for analysis. It works on recordings you made and on audio you import, and renaming a speaker once updates every mention across the transcript. Community Edition transcribes without speaker labels.

Frequently Asked Questions
What audio formats does Meetily support for import?
Meetily supports common audio formats including MP3, WAV, M4A, OGG, and FLAC. The import feature processes audio locally using the same Whisper or Parakeet transcription engines used for live meeting recording. All processing happens on your device.
Is Meetily v0.3.0 free?
Yes. Meetily Community Edition v0.3.0 is free and open source under the MIT license. Audio import, retranscription, and all core features are included at no cost. Pro features like speaker diarization and auto-detect meetings are available at $10/user/month billed annually.
Does audio import send my files to the cloud?
No. Audio import uses the same local-first architecture as live transcription. Your audio files are processed entirely on your device using Whisper or Parakeet models. Nothing is uploaded. For AI summaries after transcription, you choose: local models (fully offline) or your own API key.
What is the latest version of Meetily?
The latest Community Edition is v0.3.0, released March 3, 2026. It includes audio file import, retranscription, improved transcription quality via parallel resampling, and memory optimizations. Download it from meetily.ai/downloads or the GitHub releases page.
How does retranscription work in Meetily?
Retranscription lets you re-process an existing recording with different transcription settings. For example, you can retranscribe a meeting originally processed with the tiny Whisper model using the large model for higher accuracy. The original recording must still be available on your device.
Can Meetily transcribe audio files in languages other than English?
Yes. Meetily supports 99+ languages for transcription, including Spanish, French, German, Japanese, Chinese, Arabic, Hindi, and many more. The import feature supports the same languages as live transcription. Language is auto-detected or can be set manually.
What is the best open source meeting transcription app in 2026?
Meetily is the leading open source meeting transcription app with 31,000+ GitHub stars and 599,000+ downloads. It offers 100% local transcription with no meeting bots, pluggable AI summaries (local or BYOK), and GDPR/HIPAA compliance by design. Available for Windows and macOS under the MIT license.
Is speaker diarization available in Meetily?
Yes. Speaker diarization shipped in Meetily Pro 1.8.2. It identifies and labels different speakers in meeting transcripts and imported audio files - live as you record and on audio you re-transcribe - and runs on-device. Rename a speaker once and it updates across the transcript.

Frequently Asked Questions
Can I get AI meeting summaries without sending my audio anywhere?
Yes. Meetily transcribes your meeting locally on your device with Whisper, so your audio never leaves your machine. For the summary, you choose: a local model keeps everything on-device, or BYOK sends only the text transcript (not audio) to a provider you control.
Do I need an API key to use Meetily?
Not necessarily. You can run summaries entirely with a local model via Ollama or the built-in option, which needs no API key and keeps everything on your device. If you want a frontier model instead, you can bring your own API key (BYOK).
What is BYOK and what gets sent to the provider?
BYOK means Bring Your Own Key - you connect your own API key for a provider like Claude, OpenAI, Groq, or OpenRouter, or point Meetily at any OpenAI-compatible endpoint. Only the text transcript is sent to that provider for summarization. Your audio recording never leaves your device.
Can I still keep everything fully on-device?
Yes. Use a local model via Ollama or the built-in option for summaries. In that mode nothing leaves your device - not the audio and not the transcript. This is the recommended path for regulated industries, air-gapped environments, or any highly sensitive meeting.
Is Meetily suitable for sensitive meetings (HIPAA, legal, government)?
For regulated industries or highly sensitive meetings, use a local model so nothing leaves your device at all. BYOK sends only the text transcript (never audio) to the provider you choose. Pick the option that matches your organization's compliance requirements. Meetily is GDPR and HIPAA compliant by design.
What is an AI meeting summary tool?
An AI meeting summary tool automatically converts your meeting recordings into structured notes with key decisions, action items, and discussion points. Meetily is a privacy-first AI meeting assistant that transcribes locally and offers pluggable AI for summaries - a local model that stays fully on-device, or your own API key where only the transcript is sent.
How does Meetily compare to other AI meeting note takers?
Unlike cloud-based assistants like Otter.ai or Fireflies, Meetily keeps transcription local and never sends a bot to your calls. Your audio is never uploaded. You get summaries either fully on-device with a local model, or with frontier-model quality via your own API key where only the transcript travels - so you keep full control over where your data goes.

Frequently Asked Questions
What is Meetily?
Meetily is a privacy-first AI meeting assistant that transcribes and summarizes your meetings with 100% local processing. Unlike cloud-based tools, your audio and transcripts stay on your device. It's open source (MIT license) with over 599,000 downloads.
Is Meetily free?
The Community Edition is free and open source. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually and adds advanced exports (PDF, Word), meeting detection, and faster cloud summarization. Enterprise has custom pricing for self-hosted deployments.
Does Meetily work offline?
Yes. Transcription runs entirely on your device using Whisper or Parakeet models. After the initial model download, Meetily works completely offline with no internet required.
Is Meetily HIPAA compliant?
Meetily is HIPAA compliant by design. All processing happens locally on your device, and we never have access to your meeting content. For healthcare organizations needing formal compliance documentation, Enterprise includes custom compliance frameworks.
What platforms does Meetily support?
Meetily runs natively on Windows and macOS (Apple Silicon). It works with any meeting platform - Zoom, Teams, Meet, Discord, or any other app that produces audio - because it captures system audio rather than joining as a bot.
Does Meetily use a meeting bot?
No. Meetily captures system audio directly from your device, so there's no bot joining your calls. Other participants won't know you're transcribing, and you don't need to invite anything to your meetings.

Frequently Asked Questions
Can I still use Meetily completely locally?
Yes. Meetily Community Edition runs 100% locally. You can use Ollama or our bundled Gemma models for summarization. No cloud connection required after initial setup. The quality trade-offs we described apply, but for users who need complete data sovereignty, the local path remains available.
Which cloud provider has the best privacy policy?
For pure privacy, Groq with Zero Data Retention enabled is strong - they don't retain data by default. Anthropic Claude and OpenAI both don't train on API data. Google Gemini's free tier does train on your data, so be careful there. For maximum control, self-hosted OpenWebUI is best.
Why not just wait for local models to improve?
We're actively tracking this space. Local models improve rapidly. But today, users need working solutions. We'd rather give you options with transparency than make you wait indefinitely or use subpar quality in the meantime.
What data gets sent to cloud providers?
Only the text transcript - never the audio. You can also choose to only send portions of the transcript. The raw meeting recording always stays on your device.
Does Meetily make money from cloud API usage?
When you bring your own API key, you pay the provider directly. We don't mark up costs or take a cut. And local models run on your own hardware at no per-summary cost at all.

Frequently Asked Questions
Is Meetily free to use?
Yes. Meetily Community Edition is completely free and open source under the MIT license. You can download it from meetily.ai/downloads or build from source on GitHub. Pro is $10/user/month billed annually for enhanced features.
Does Meetily require an internet connection?
No. Both Community and Pro run 100% locally on your device. Internet is only needed to download the app, for optional updates, and if you choose to use external AI model providers for summary generation.
Can I install both Community and Pro?
Yes. Community and Pro are separate applications that can be installed side by side on the same machine.
What happens when my Pro trial expires?
After 14 days, Pro features will be disabled. Your recordings and transcripts remain on your device. You can purchase a Pro license to continue using Pro features, or switch to the free Community Edition.
Does Meetily work with Zoom, Teams, and Google Meet?
Yes. Meetily captures system audio directly, so it works with any meeting platform including Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, Webex, and Slack Huddles. No bot joins your meeting.
Is my meeting data sent to any server?
No. All transcription and summarization happens locally on your device. Your audio and transcripts never leave your computer. This is true for both Community and Pro editions.
What are the system requirements?
macOS 11.0+ with Apple Silicon (M1/M2/M3/M4), or Windows 10+ with x64 processor. Minimum 8 GB RAM recommended, 16 GB for best performance. About 2 GB of free storage space.
How do I activate my Pro license key?
Open Meetily Pro, go to Settings, then Pro, and paste your license key. Click Activate. Your license key is sent to the email you used during purchase or trial signup.

Are AI meeting assistants safe to use?
It depends on the tool. Cloud-based AI meeting assistants like Otter.ai, Fireflies, and Read AI have faced lawsuits and institutional bans over privacy concerns. Local AI tools like Meetily that process data on your device are architecturally safer because your data never leaves your control.
Is Otter AI safe?
Otter.ai is currently facing a class action lawsuit (Brewer v. Otter.ai, August 2025) alleging recording without consent and using data to train AI without permission. Otter has SOC 2 certification and achieved HIPAA compliance in July 2025, but the consent model-where the bot auto-joins and seeks host consent only-remains legally contested in all-party consent states.
Is Fireflies AI safe?
Fireflies.ai faces a December 2025 lawsuit alleging illegal biometric data collection under Illinois BIPA. Users have reported the bot continuing to join meetings even after account deletion. The tool processes all data on cloud servers, creating exposure if Fireflies is breached.
Why was Read AI banned by universities?
Chapman University banned Read AI in August 2025 citing security, privacy, and institutional data risks. The tool had concerning behaviors like auto-joining meetings and following users across platforms. Universities must protect student data under FERPA and institutional policies that cloud AI tools often violate.
What is the safest AI meeting assistant?
The safest AI meeting assistants use local processing, meaning data never leaves your device. Meetily is the leading option-its open source (MIT license), bot-free, works offline, and keeps all data on your machine. For cloud tools, platform-native options (Microsoft Copilot, Zoom AI) avoid third-party data exposure but still use cloud processing.
Do AI meeting tools violate wiretapping laws?
Potentially. 12 US states require all-party consent before recording: California, Florida, Illinois, Maryland, Massachusetts, Michigan, Montana, New Hampshire, Pennsylvania, Washington, Delaware, and Connecticut. Cloud meeting tools that auto-join and seek only host consent may violate these laws-which is exactly what the Otter.ai lawsuit alleges.
Can my employer record meetings with AI without telling me?
It depends on state law and company policy. In one-party consent states, employers can record if they participate. In two-party consent states, all participants must be informed. Either way, most workplace policies require disclosure. AI meeting tools should announce their presence-but not all do clearly.
How do I protect my privacy in AI-recorded meetings?
Ask if the meeting is being recorded and by what tool. Request recording be turned off for sensitive topics. In sensitive industries, advocate for local AI tools that dont upload data to third parties. Review your organizations approved tools list-shadow AI is a major breach risk.

Frequently Asked Questions
What's the best app for recording AND transcribing meetings in 2026?
It depends on whether a bot in the call is acceptable. If not, Meetily captures system audio locally on Windows or macOS, transcribes with OpenAI Whisper or Parakeet, and never adds a visible bot to your Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams call - Community Edition is free and open source under MIT; Pro is $10/user/month billed annually. If a visible bot is fine and you want zero setup, Fathom is the strongest cloud option with unlimited free recording plus transcription. Otter.ai and Fireflies also bundle recording with transcription and add real-time collaboration, but route audio through their cloud.
What's the best free AI meeting note taker for teams?
Two genuinely free choices stand out for teams in 2026. Meetily's Community Edition is fully free and open source (MIT) - with thousands of GitHub stars and a six-figure download count - offering local Whisper transcription, AI summaries, and bot-free recording. Fathom offers unlimited free cloud recording and transcription (AI summaries capped at 5/month on the free plan). Otter.ai and Fireflies have free tiers but cap them tightly (300 minutes/month on Otter Basic; 800 minutes/seat on Fireflies Free). For privacy-sensitive teams, Meetily is the only free option that keeps recordings on-device.
Read AI vs Granola: which should I pick in 2026?
Pick Granola if you want a polished, bot-free note-taker (Windows & Mac) that listens to device audio. Pick Read AI only if you specifically want meeting-engagement analytics and are comfortable with a visible bot that auto-joins from calendar sync. Granola: bot-free, hybrid local capture, $18/month individual, 25 free meetings lifetime. Read AI: cloud bot, engagement scores, free tier with caps, $25/user/month enterprise. If fully on-device processing matters more than either, a local-first tool like Meetily (free, open source) is also worth a look.
Which AI meeting assistants are most affordable for startups?
For lean startups, the most affordable AI meeting assistants in 2026 are: (1) Meetily Community Edition - free, open source, MIT-licensed, no per-seat fees; (2) Fathom Free - unlimited cloud recording and transcription with 5 AI summaries/month; (3) Meetily Pro - $10/user/month billed annually for speaker identification and advanced exports; (4) Otter Pro at $8-10/user/month on annual plans. Most expensive: Microsoft 365 Copilot at $30/user/month on top of an existing M365 subscription. If the team uses Mac and Windows, Meetily, Fathom, and Granola all run on both.
How do AI meeting assistants compare in 2026?
The 8-way 2026 comparison turns on four axes: bot visibility (Meetily and Granola are bot-free; Otter, Fireflies, Fathom, Read AI add a visible bot; Copilot and Zoom AI are platform-native), data location (Meetily processes locally; everyone else uploads to vendor cloud), price (Meetily Community is free, Pro is $10/user/month billed annually; Otter Pro $8-10/mo annual; Fireflies $10/mo; Granola $18/mo; Copilot $30/user/month), and platform reach (Meetily is cross-platform; Copilot is Teams-only; Zoom AI is Zoom-only). See the comparison table earlier in this guide for a side-by-side.
What is an AI note taker and how does it work?
An AI note taker is software that captures meeting audio and uses speech-to-text plus a language model to produce a transcript, summary, and action items automatically. Two architectures exist in 2026: (1) bot-based - the tool dials in as a visible participant (Otter, Fireflies, Fathom, Read AI) and uploads audio to vendor cloud; (2) bot-free local capture - the tool records system audio on the host's device (Meetily, Granola) and may transcribe on-device or in the cloud. Meetily runs the entire pipeline locally with OpenAI Whisper, so audio and transcripts stay on your machine unless you bring your own API key for the summary step.
Which meeting note tools have unlimited usage?
Truly unlimited in 2026: (1) Meetily Community Edition - no per-meeting, per-minute, or per-month caps because everything runs locally; (2) Fathom Free - unlimited cloud recording and transcription, but only 5 AI summaries per month on free, unlimited on Premium ($15-19/mo); (3) Meetily Pro - unlimited meetings included with the $10/user/month billed annually plan. Capped tools: Otter Basic (300 min/month), Fireflies Free (800 min/seat), Granola Free (25 meetings lifetime), Read AI Free (5 meetings/month). For organizations that record dozens of meetings per week, local-first processing is the only architecture with no metered ceiling, because there is no per-minute cloud cost to pass on.
What is the best meeting notes software in 2026?
The best meeting notes software depends on your priorities. For privacy and local processing, Meetily leads as an open-source AI meeting assistant. For enterprise collaboration, Otter.ai and Fireflies offer robust cloud features. For budget-conscious teams, Fathom provides unlimited free transcription.
Is there a free AI meeting assistant?
Yes, several options exist. Meetily's Community Edition is completely free and open source with core features included. Fathom offers unlimited free transcription (genuinely unlimited, not a trial). Otter.ai, Fireflies, and Read AI have free tiers with monthly limits. Microsoft Copilot is included if you already pay for Microsoft 365 Copilot.
What is the best meeting AI for privacy?
For privacy, the strongest architecture is local processing, and Meetily is the only tool in this comparison that runs the full pipeline on-device: audio and transcripts stay on your machine, and with local summarization it works completely offline - well suited to HIPAA and GDPR requirements. (If you bring your own API key, only transcript text is sent to your chosen provider for the summary step, and only if you choose that.) Among cloud tools, Granola keeps capture local but processes in the cloud; the rest upload audio to their servers.
Do AI meeting assistants join as bots?
Most do. Otter.ai, Fireflies, Fathom, and Read AI all join meetings as visible 'Notetaker' bots that other participants can see. Meetily is bot-free - it records system audio locally without joining the meeting. Platform-native tools (Microsoft Copilot, Zoom AI) also avoid external bots.
Can meeting notes software work with Zoom, Teams, and Google Meet?
Most meeting notes software supports all major platforms. Meetily works with any platform because it captures system audio locally. Otter.ai, Fireflies, and Fathom have dedicated integrations for Zoom, Teams, and Meet. Microsoft Copilot only works in Teams, and Zoom AI only works in Zoom.
Is AI meeting transcription accurate?
Yes, modern AI meeting assistants achieve high transcription accuracy on clear English audio. Meetily uses OpenAI Whisper which supports 99+ languages. Cloud tools like Otter.ai and Fireflies have similar accuracy. All tools struggle with heavy accents, background noise, or multiple overlapping speakers.
What meeting notes software is HIPAA compliant?
Meetily is HIPAA-compliant by design: transcription always runs locally on your device, so audio recordings and Protected Health Information are never transmitted for the recording step. For summaries, Meetily uses pluggable AI - choose local AI (Ollama or built-in models), or bring your own API key. For strict HIPAA workflows, Community Edition or local-only summarization keeps PHI entirely on-device with no BAA needed. Enterprise teams can request a BAA. Otter.ai achieved HIPAA compliance in July 2025 and Fireflies launched 'Fireflies for Healthcare' in September 2025, both on Enterprise plans with BAA requests required. Local processing with Meetily eliminates third-party PHI exposure entirely.
How much does meeting notes software cost?
Prices vary widely. Meetily Community is free (open source) with Pro at $10/user/month billed annually ($120/year). Fathom offers unlimited free recordings (AI summaries limited). Annual plans are typically 40% cheaper: Otter Pro ($8-10/mo), Fireflies Pro ($10/mo), Fathom Premium ($15-19/mo). Monthly rates are higher: Otter ($17/mo), Fireflies ($18/mo), Granola ($18/mo). Enterprise options like Microsoft Copilot add $30/user/month on top of existing M365 subscriptions.

Frequently Asked Questions
What's the best self-hosted meeting transcription tool in 2026?
Meetily is the best self-hosted meeting transcription tool in 2026 because it combines bot-free meeting recording with Whisper-powered transcription and AI summaries in one package, and runs entirely on your own hardware or infrastructure. If you only need a raw transcription engine, [OpenAI Whisper](https://github.com/openai/whisper) (MIT-licensed since September 2022) is the accuracy standard - but you build the meeting workflow yourself. For team-scale deployment with admin controls, Meetily Enterprise can be self-hosted on your own servers.
How do I self-host transcription for meetings?
The fastest path to self-hosted meeting transcription in 2026: (1) Install Meetily Community Edition on each user's Windows or macOS machine - it captures system audio locally and runs Whisper on-device with no server needed; (2) For team-scale, deploy Meetily Enterprise on your own infrastructure for centralized transcripts and admin controls; (3) For a build-your-own pipeline, run OpenAI Whisper (MIT) on a GPU host with an audio-capture front end. Self-hosting means no audio leaves the perimeter you control, which removes the GDPR cross-border transfer concern that cloud meeting AI introduces.
Which open-source tools support meeting transcription?
Open-source meeting-transcription tools in 2026 cluster into two groups. Full meeting workflow (recording + transcription + summaries): Meetily (MIT), and a handful of community forks. Transcription engines only (you build the meeting workflow): OpenAI Whisper (MIT), Whisper.cpp (MIT), Faster Whisper (MIT), WhisperX (BSD-style with diarization), NVIDIA NeMo (Apache 2.0), Vosk (Apache 2.0), and Mozilla DeepSpeech (deprecated; use Coqui STT). Most cloud meeting-AI tools (Otter, Fireflies, Fathom, Read AI) are proprietary and cannot be self-hosted.
Does Meetily support speaker diarization?
Speaker diarization is in active development at Meetily - a POC is available and WhisperX-style integration can be configured for advanced setups. When enabled, diarization runs locally alongside Whisper or Parakeet transcription, so speaker labels are produced without sending audio to any cloud service. Accuracy improves with clearer audio and fewer overlapping speakers, and is currently strongest in English. Diarization quality is improving with each Meetily release.
Can I run Meetily in Docker?
Meetily ships as a desktop app for Windows and macOS, so individual users typically install the native installer rather than a container. The Community Edition can be built from source and run in containers for self-hosted setups - the repo at github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily contains the necessary source under MIT. Enterprise customers can discuss custom deployments by booking a demo at /enterprise. Linux desktop support (native) is on the roadmap; for now Linux users can build the open-source app bundle from source.
Which AI note takers store transcripts locally instead of on vendor servers?
Local-storage AI note takers in 2026: (1) Meetily - transcripts and audio default to your device with no cloud sync unless you opt in; (2) Self-built Whisper or Whisper.cpp pipelines store wherever you point them; (3) Vosk and NVIDIA NeMo similarly store output locally. Cloud-only (transcripts on vendor servers): Otter.ai, Fireflies.ai, Fathom, Read AI, tl;dv, Microsoft Copilot, Zoom AI Companion, Granola (note: Granola is bot-free for capture but transcripts are processed and stored in their cloud). If on-device storage is a hard requirement, Meetily is the only tool that ships this out of the box with a polished meeting workflow.
Meetily vs WhisperX: which should I pick?
Pick Meetily if you want a complete meeting app: bot-free recording, Whisper transcription, speaker diarization, AI summaries, and a polished UI - all running locally on Windows or macOS, free under MIT. Pick WhisperX (BSD-style, github.com/m-bain/whisperX) if you want a transcription-and-diarization library to embed in your own pipeline and you are comfortable wiring it into your audio capture and workflow yourself. Meetily uses Whisper under the hood and can be plugged with WhisperX-style diarization for advanced cases; for most teams, Meetily delivers a working product without engineering work.
Can I self-host Otter.ai or Fireflies?
No. Otter.ai and Fireflies are cloud-only services with no self-hosted option. Both faced 2025 lawsuits over their data practices: Otter.ai was sued in California federal court (Brewer v. Otter.ai, August 2025) for recording without all-party consent, and Fireflies.ai was sued under Illinois BIPA in December 2025 for collecting voiceprints. If you need self-hosted meeting transcription, you must use open source alternatives like Meetily, Whisper, or Vosk.
Is self-hosted transcription GDPR compliant?
Yes. Self-hosted transcription is inherently GDPR compliant because no personal data is transferred to third parties. Your audio stays on your infrastructure, eliminating cross-border data transfer concerns and third-party processor agreements. There is no Data Processing Agreement to negotiate because there is no processor. Meetily's Community Edition is open source under MIT, so you can also audit the code path that handles audio.
Can I run an AI note taker that keeps transcripts on my own servers, not on a vendor's?
Yes. Meetily, Whisper, and the other tools in this guide are designed exactly for this. Meetily captures system audio on your laptop, transcribes locally with Whisper or Parakeet, and stores transcripts on your device by default - vendor servers are never in the loop. For team deployments, Meetily Enterprise can be self-hosted on your own infrastructure with admin controls. Cloud meeting AI products like Otter.ai, Fireflies, and Read AI store transcripts on their servers and cannot be self-hosted.
How accurate is self-hosted transcription?
Self-hosted tools using Whisper achieve high accuracy on clear English audio, matching or exceeding cloud services in published benchmarks. Whisper supports 99+ languages. Lighter tools like Vosk have lower accuracy but run on minimal hardware including Raspberry Pi. Meetily runs the same Whisper and Parakeet engines in its free Community edition and in Pro.
Do I need a GPU for self-hosted transcription?
Not necessarily. Whisper and most tools work on CPU, just slower. Whisper-tiny runs in real-time on modern CPUs. For faster processing, an NVIDIA GPU helps significantly - Faster Whisper and NVIDIA NeMo are both optimized for CUDA. Vosk is optimized for CPU and works on Raspberry Pi for edge deployments.
Can self-hosted tools do speaker identification?
Yes. WhisperX adds speaker diarization to Whisper transcription, identifying which speaker said what. Meetily also supports speaker identification. This requires additional processing but works entirely self-hosted - no audio is sent to a cloud service for diarization.
What hardware do I need for self-hosted transcription?
Minimum: Modern quad-core CPU, 8GB RAM, 5GB storage. Recommended: 16GB+ RAM, NVIDIA GPU (any recent model), SSD. For real-time transcription, more powerful hardware helps. Vosk can run on Raspberry Pi for offline use. Meetily desktop runs on Windows and macOS (M Series); for Linux, build from the open source repo.
Is self-hosted transcription free?
Yes, most self-hosted transcription tools are free and open source. Meetily Community Edition (MIT), Whisper (MIT), Vosk (Apache 2.0), and others cost nothing to use beyond your own hardware and setup time. Meetily Pro is a paid upgrade ($10/user/month billed annually) that adds speaker identification, custom summary templates, and advanced exports. In both editions, transcription always runs locally on your machine. For summaries, you choose: a local LLM (Ollama or built-in) that keeps everything on-device, or your own API key (Claude, OpenAI, Groq). With BYOK, only transcript text is sent to your chosen provider, never audio or recordings.

Frequently Asked Questions
What is a self-hosted meeting note taker?
A self-hosted meeting note taker runs entirely on your own infrastructure (laptop, server, or data center) rather than uploading audio to cloud services. Your meeting recordings and notes never leave your control, providing complete data privacy and sovereignty.
Is Meetily really self-hosted?
Yes. Meetily runs 100% locally on your device. The AI transcription (Whisper or Parakeet) processes audio on your hardware, notes are stored on your drives, and no data is ever uploaded to any cloud service. You can run it completely offline after initial setup.
Can I self-host Otter.ai or Fireflies?
No. Otter.ai and Fireflies are cloud-only services with no self-hosted option. They require uploading meeting audio to their servers for processing. If you need self-hosted meeting notes, you must use open source alternatives like Meetily.
Do I need a powerful computer for self-hosted meeting notes?
Not necessarily. Meetily works on any modern computer. For faster transcription, a GPU helps (NVIDIA recommended), but CPU-only processing works fine - just slower. Minimum: quad-core CPU, 8GB RAM. Recommended: 8-core CPU, 16GB RAM, NVIDIA GPU.
Can a self-hosted meeting note taker work offline?
Yes. Once set up, Meetily works completely offline. The AI models run locally, so no internet connection is needed for recording, transcription, or note generation. This is ideal for air-gapped environments or unreliable connections.
How accurate is self-hosted transcription?
Very accurate. Meetily uses OpenAI Whisper which achieves high accuracy on clear English audio and supports 99+ languages. This matches or exceeds cloud services. Accuracy depends on audio quality and model size selected.
Is self-hosted meeting notes HIPAA compliant?
Self-hosting is inherently more HIPAA-friendly because no PHI leaves your infrastructure. There are no third-party processors, no BAAs required with vendors, and you control all access. However, you're still responsible for implementing proper security controls.
What's the cost of self-hosted meeting notes?
Meetily's Community Edition is free and open source. Your costs are hardware (use existing computer or server), electricity, and your time for setup and maintenance. Pro edition is available at $10/user/month billed annually for enhanced features. For high-volume users, self-hosting is dramatically cheaper than cloud subscriptions.

Frequently Asked Questions
Which meeting apps allow free recording in 2026?
Three apps allow free meeting recording without a monthly minute cap in 2026: (1) Meetily Community Edition - free, open source under MIT, records and transcribes locally on Windows and macOS with no monthly caps; (2) Fathom Free - unlimited cloud recordings, transcripts and storage (advanced AI summaries on the first 5 calls each month); (3) OBS Studio - free, open source under GPLv2, records anything on screen but requires audio-routing setup and has no transcription. Capped free tiers, checked October 11, 2026: Otter Basic (300 minutes/month, 30 minutes per conversation, 3 lifetime imports), Fireflies Free (400 minutes of storage per seat, 20 one-time AI credits), Granola Free (30 days of meeting history), tl;dv Free (unlimited recordings, but full AI notes only for your first 10 meetings and 5 file uploads, for the life of the account), Loom Starter (25 videos, 5-minute recordings). Zoom's free plan records locally and adds 3 AI summaries a month through ZoomMate Basic.
How do I record a meeting for free?
On Windows or macOS, the fastest free path is: (1) Install Meetily Community Edition (free, open source) from meetily.ai; (2) Pick the Whisper or Parakeet transcription engine in setup; (3) Start the recording before joining your Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams call. Meetily captures system audio and microphone locally without joining the meeting as a bot, so other participants see no notetaker bot. The recording and transcript stay on your machine, and summaries use a local model or your own API key. If you want a cloud option, Fathom Free records in the cloud and joins as a visible bot unless you use its Mac-only bot-free mode. Our guide on how to record a meeting covers each platform step by step, including consent.
What's the best free meeting recorder AND transcriber?
For free recording plus AI transcription in one app, Meetily Community Edition is the strongest pick if you want audio kept on your device: it captures system audio locally (no bot in the call), transcribes with OpenAI Whisper or Parakeet across 99+ languages, and stores everything on-device under the MIT license. Fathom Free is the strongest cloud alternative - unlimited transcription, but audio is processed in Fathom's cloud and its bot-free mode is Mac-only. OBS Studio records audio but does not transcribe. Of the ten tools in this guide, Meetily, OBS Studio and Zoom's local recording keep the recording file on your device, and only Meetily adds on-device transcription. OpenWhispr and Anarlog are other open-source local options outside the ranked list.
What recording devices work best for in-person meetings?
For in-person meetings, the recording stack splits into hardware and software. Hardware: a dedicated USB conference mic (Jabra Speak 750, Anker PowerConf S330, or a Blue Yeti) placed centrally on the table captures all speakers cleanly. Phone-based options like an iPhone laid flat work in a pinch. Software: route the mic into a local recorder like Meetily on a laptop and let Whisper transcribe in real time. Meetily works the same way for in-person as for online meetings because it captures whatever audio your system hears. Bot-based cloud tools cannot record an in-person room by joining a call; some offer a separate mobile or desktop app for that, which sends the audio to the vendor's cloud.
Best meeting recorder and transcriber in 2026?
The best meeting recorder and transcriber in 2026 depends on whether you need the data to stay local. Best local-first: Meetily - free Community Edition under MIT, no bot, Whisper or Parakeet transcription, works offline. Best cloud free plan: Fathom - unlimited recordings and transcripts, with advanced AI summaries on five calls a month. Best for sales teams: Fireflies.ai with Salesforce/HubSpot CRM sync ($10/user/month billed annually on Pro). Best for Microsoft 365 shops: Microsoft 365 Copilot, an add-on license on top of a qualifying Microsoft 365 plan. Best raw recorder without transcription: OBS Studio.
What's the best meeting recording app?
The best meeting recording app for people who want recordings to stay private in 2026 is Meetily: it records Zoom, Google Meet, Microsoft Teams, Discord, and Webex calls locally on Windows or macOS (Apple Silicon), adds no bot to the meeting, and the Community Edition is free under the MIT license. It bundles AI transcription (Whisper or Parakeet) and AI summaries in the same app, so you do not need a separate transcriber. For sales-heavy workflows with CRM dependency, Fireflies.ai is the conventional pick. For unlimited free cloud recording, Fathom is the alternative. None of the other apps in this guide combine bot-free, local, and free in one package.
What app should I use to record meetings?
Match the app to the constraint. Privacy or compliance constraint (healthcare, legal, financial, regulated industries): Meetily Community Edition - local recording, no audio upload, MIT-licensed. Cost constraint with unlimited free needed: Meetily or Fathom Free. Workflow constraint (CRM sync, sales coaching): Fireflies.ai. Microsoft 365 constraint: Microsoft 365 Copilot inside Teams. Polished notes app: Granola (captures audio on your device without a bot but processes notes in the cloud; the free plan keeps 30 days of history and Business is $14/user/month). For most people who want privacy, low cost, and no bot together, Meetily covers all three.
Top meeting recording tools in 2026?
Our ranked top 10 meeting recording tools for 2026, ordered by the criteria in this guide (where audio is stored first, then bot use and free-tier limits), with primary trade-offs: (1) Meetily - local, no bot, Community Edition free under MIT; (2) Fathom Free - cloud, unlimited free recordings and transcription, bot by default (bot-free on Mac); (3) OBS Studio - powerful local recorder, no transcription, steep setup; (4) Otter.ai - cloud, capped free tier, bot or desktop app, subject of a pending privacy class action; (5) Fireflies.ai - cloud, CRM-friendly, capped free tier, a pending BIPA lawsuit; (6) tl;dv - cloud, free recordings but lifetime-capped AI notes, multi-meeting AI reports; (7) Zoom local recording - Zoom-only, ZoomMate Basic adds 3 summaries a month; (8) Google Meet recording - needs Workspace Business Standard or higher; (9) Microsoft Teams recording - needs a paid Microsoft 365 plan; (10) Loom - async video, free Starter plan limited to 25 videos.
Is Meetily really free?
Yes. Meetily's Community Edition is free and open source under the MIT license, with no monthly minute or meeting caps. It includes core features like real-time transcription, AI summaries, and local recording. Pro features like speaker identification, auto-detect meetings, and advanced exports are available at $10/user/month billed annually. The core recorder is free.
Does Meetily work with Zoom, Teams, and Google Meet?
Yes, Meetily records any meeting platform because it captures your system audio and microphone. It's platform-agnostic and works with Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, Discord, Slack Huddles, Webex, and any other platform that outputs audio through your computer.
Can other meeting participants see Meetily recording?
No bot appears in the call. Meetily is bot-free, which means it doesn't add any visible participant to your meetings. Fireflies, Otter.ai, Fathom and tl;dv join as a visible Notetaker bot by default (each also has a desktop mode that records without one), while Meetily captures audio locally. Only you see that it is recording. You should still follow proper consent laws and inform participants when recording.
Where are my recordings stored?
All recordings are stored locally on your computer in a folder you choose. Audio is never uploaded to a cloud service. You can back the files up, encrypt them, or delete them anytime. If you use your own API key for summaries, the transcript text is sent to the provider you chose; a local summary model keeps summaries on your device too. This fits GDPR, HIPAA, and other compliance requirements by design, but confirm your own obligations with your compliance team.
Does Meetily require internet to work?
No. Once installed and with AI models downloaded, recording and transcription work completely offline. The AI transcription runs on your local machine using Whisper or Parakeet engines. Only the initial download requires internet, and summaries stay offline only with a local model (an API key needs a connection). This suits air-gapped environments and unreliable connections.
How accurate is Meetily's transcription?
Meetily uses OpenAI's Whisper model, which delivers high accuracy on clear audio in English, and supports 99+ languages. The Large v3 model provides the best accuracy, while smaller models (Base, Small) offer faster performance on older hardware.
What are the system requirements for Meetily?
Minimum: a Windows PC or an Apple Silicon Mac with 8GB RAM, a 4-core CPU, and 10GB storage. Recommended: 16GB RAM, an 8-core CPU, 50GB storage, GPU acceleration (NVIDIA, AMD, or Apple Silicon), and an SSD. The Whisper Tiny model works on older hardware, while larger models benefit from newer systems.
Is Meetily GDPR and HIPAA compliant?
By design, not by certification. Transcription runs on your device, so audio is not sent to a third party, which helps with EU data residency and HIPAA requirements. If you choose your own API key for summaries, that provider receives the transcript text and belongs in your compliance review; a local summary model avoids this. Meetily does not hold formal GDPR or HIPAA certifications, and the open-source code allows security audits. Check your own obligations with your compliance team.
How can I record a meeting without using Zoom's built-in recorder?
Use a standalone meeting recorder like Meetily that captures your system audio directly. Install it on your computer, start the recording before your Zoom call, and it will capture everything without using Zoom's native recording. This means no recording notification is sent to participants (though you should still inform them per consent laws), and your recording stays on your local device instead of Zoom's cloud servers.
Which meeting recorders work for both in-person and online meetings?
Meetily and OBS Studio both work for in-person meetings because they capture audio from your device's microphone. For in-person meetings, place your laptop in the room and Meetily will record and transcribe using its AI engine. For online meetings, it captures system audio. Cloud tools like Otter.ai and Fireflies join online meetings with a bot by default and offer separate desktop or mobile apps for other situations; their audio goes to the vendor's cloud.
Are there meeting recorders that don't store audio in the cloud?
Yes. Meetily stores everything locally on your computer with no audio uploads. OBS Studio also records locally. Zoom offers local recording (not cloud) on its free tier. All other tools in this list - Fathom, Otter.ai, Fireflies.ai, and tl;dv - upload your meeting audio to cloud servers for processing, including in their bot-free desktop modes.

Introduction: The Bot-Free Revolution

If you've used Fireflies.ai, you've experienced this:

"Fireflies Notetaker has joined the meeting"

That moment when your client sees the recording bot. The awkward pause. The "are we being recorded?" question. The scramble to explain your AI note-taking tool.

What if the bot simply didn't exist?

That's the promise of bot-free meeting transcription. Meetily captures your meetings invisibly-no bots, no announcements, no disruption-while giving you the same AI-powered transcription and summaries.

But there's a tradeoff: Fireflies is plug-and-play cloud convenience. Meetily requires local installation and processing. Which is right for you?

This comprehensive comparison answers that question.
Quick Comparison Table
Feature	Meetily	Fireflies.ai
Pricing	Free Community + $10/user/month Pro (billed annually) + Custom Enterprise	$10-39/month (Pro to Enterprise)
Bot Visibility	✗ Bot-free (invisible)	✓ Visible bot joins
Auto Meeting Detection	✓ Pro tier (hardware-level)	✓ Calendar integration
Processing	100% Local on device	Cloud-based
Data Storage	Local only (default)	Fireflies cloud
Privacy	No data leaves device	Uploaded to Fireflies servers
Platform	Desktop app (Win/Mac)	Web app + Mobile apps
Works Offline	✓ Yes	✗ Requires internet
Open Source	✓ MIT License (31,094+ stars)	✗ Proprietary
Multi-Language	✓ 99+ languages	✓ 69+ languages

The Bottom Line:

• Choose Fireflies if you want instant setup, mobile apps, and extensive integrations-and you're comfortable with cloud storage
• Choose Meetily if you prioritize privacy, bot-free recording, local processing, or work in regulated industries (HIPAA, GDPR)
Privacy & Data Security: The #1 Differentiator

This is where the philosophical difference between the two tools becomes stark.

Fireflies.ai: Cloud-Based Processing

How it works:

Fireflies bot joins your meeting
Audio is streamed to Fireflies servers
Transcription happens on Fireflies infrastructure (AWS)
Transcript and audio are stored on Fireflies cloud
You access via Fireflies web app

Privacy Concerns:

Third-party processor: Your meeting data is handled by Fireflies
HIPAA Compliance: Only available for Enterprise plan customers ($39/user/month) with signed BAA-not available for Free, Pro, or Business plans
Cloud storage: Audio recordings and transcripts live on Fireflies servers
US jurisdiction: Data subject to US laws (CLOUD Act, etc.)
Data access: Fireflies employees have technical access to your data
Meetily: Local-First Privacy

How it works:

You start recording on your device (Community Edition: manual start, Pro: automatic detection)
Audio is captured from your system (never uploaded)
Transcription happens on YOUR computer (Whisper or Parakeet engine)
Transcript is saved to YOUR local storage
Optional: Use local LLM (Ollama) or bring your own API key for summaries

Privacy Benefits:

Local transcription: Audio and transcripts stay on your device
Pluggable AI summaries: Choose local models or your own API key
GDPR compliant by design: Transcription runs locally, you control your summary provider
HIPAA compliant by design: Use fully local processing for regulated data
No data mining: Open-source code means no hidden telemetry

Who Wins?

• Fireflies: If you trust cloud providers, want zero setup, and work in unregulated industries
• Meetily: If you work in healthcare, legal, finance, government, or anywhere data sovereignty matters
Bot Visibility: Invisible Recording vs Visible Bots
Fireflies.ai: The Visible Bot

When you use Fireflies, it joins your meeting as a participant:

Appears in participant list as "Fireflies Notetaker" or similar
Shows video thumbnail (usually Fireflies logo)
Announces "Fireflies has joined the meeting" (on some platforms)
Can be seen by all participants
Meetily: Invisible Recording

Meetily captures your computer's system audio:

No bot joins the meeting (other participants see only you)
Invisible to all meeting participants
No participant list entry, no video thumbnail
Platform-agnostic (works with ANY meeting software)

The Tradeoff: Automation vs Invisibility

Fireflies offers automatic meeting joining: Schedule Fireflies to auto-join your calendar meetings without you clicking anything.

Meetily offers two options:

• Pro: Automatically detects and records meetings (similar automation to Fireflies, but invisible)
• Community Edition: Manual start/stop recording (use keyboard shortcuts for convenience)
